## 2026-09-30: Phase 3-6a ticket 32, live verification harness

- **Built:**
  - `scripts/live-verify.sh [--only <part>] [--rehearse] [--companies …] [--limit N] [--max-chat-calls N] [--stack cluster|compose|none] [--model M] [--keep-db] [--keep-bank] [--results DIR] [--yes] [--dry-run]`. It follows `scripts/live-tests.sh`: it dies under `CI`, and without LiteLLM settings in the environment or `.env`. It reads `.env` CRLF-safe and never prints a secret. It asks before a live run, and the default stack is the cluster Hindsight. A live run also needs `ATLAS_SEC_USER_AGENT` when `sec`, `discovery` or `identity` is selected. Reports go to `.scratch/live-runs/<stamp>-verify[-rehearse]/`.
  - `tests/live/test_live_verify.py` has seven parts, one test each, in order. The preflight, fresh database, throwaway bank (deleted unless kept) and bank deletion are `tests/live/stack.py`'s. The template is applied once, and the universe (the repo's plus NVIDIA) is seeded once.
    1. `sec`: live ingest with `--limit` and `--max-retains`, and triage on. Then it waits for consolidation and runs a recall and a reflect with citation counts and the companies cited.
    2. `exchanges`: IQE on the FCA NSM and Soitec on the AMF, `--limit 1` each, with retention off. It reports gate decisions and documents.
    3. `tradingview`: a hook. It is skipped unless the build registers `tradingview_catalog`/`tradingview_transcripts` and `ATLAS_TRADINGVIEW_ENABLED` is set.
    4. `discovery`: Scout, SearXNG and leads, then `propose_candidates` with live resolution. It is skipped without SearXNG.
    5. `relationships`: `extract_claims` on the recorded Coherent 10-K, then `review_relationships`.
    6. `investigation`: runs on Coherent and Lumentum to a stop. Then a Hypothesis draft and a scenario: the Analyst's table, or an all-estimated researcher table after a 409. The scenario is recomputed and resent, and both must be byte-identical. Last, it publishes version 1 and reports whether a snapshot exists.
    7. `identity`: `atlas companies resolve` for Lumentum and Coherent, then the pending reviews.
  - `tests/live/verify.py`:
    - `CappedProxy`: a forwarding proxy on localhost that counts chat completions and retain batches per part and per run. Over a cap it answers HTTP 400, and the part is `aborted`. Its leftover jobs are then failed without retry.
    - `PART_BUDGETS`: chat 12/0/0/8/6/14/0 (sum 40); retains 25 for `sec`, 0 elsewhere.
    - `VerifyReport`: `results.json` and a `summary.md` table. Each part has a status, reason, budget, the proxies' counts, what the database recorded since the part began (`llm_call`, `role_call`, `hindsight_operation`), numbers and seconds.
    - `RehearsalModel`: scripted answers for every role.
  - Settings bound the rest: 30 sections per triage call, 8 passages per extraction, a 150,000-token run budget, 3 discovery queries, and 5 leads and 6 documents per investigation.
  - `tests/live/stack.py`: `Rehearsal` keeps its `FakeLiteLLM` (`llm`), so a suite can script chat replies. `tests/live/conftest.py` adds the suite's opt-in skip.
  - Docs: `docs/runbooks.md` "Live verification (ticket 32)" and an `AGENTS.md` command and layout line.
- **Files:** `scripts/live-verify.sh`, `tests/live/{test_live_verify,verify}.py`, `tests/live/{stack,conftest}.py`, `tests/unit/test_live_suite_guard.py`, `docs/runbooks.md`, `AGENTS.md`, this log, the ticket.
- **Tests:**
  - `scripts/live-verify.sh --rehearse` covers every part against the fakes on localhost. Those fakes are the recorded Hindsight, the scripted LiteLLM, SearXNG over `tests/fixtures/searxng`, identity over `tests/fixtures/identity`, and the EDGAR, FCA NSM and AMF fixtures. Result: **6 passed, 1 skipped** (tradingview: not in this build), 16/40 chat completions and 4/25 retain operations, about 75 s.
  - The abort was checked by hand. `ATLAS_LIVE_VERIFY_MAX_CHAT_CALLS=5`, with `sec` and `investigation` selected, left `sec` passed and `investigation` **aborted** ("LiteLLM chat cap reached").
  - `tests/unit/test_live_suite_guard.py` +2: the suite is deselected by default and skipped without the opt-in, and a live run under `CI` is refused with exit 4. Result: 7 passed.
  - The Phase 2 rehearsal still passes after the `Rehearsal` change (7 passed, 1 skipped).
  - ruff format and check (backend, tests, scripts) and strict pyright are clean. The harness was written with its rehearsal, not test-first; it is test infrastructure, not a product seam.
- **Fixture-only vs live:** **nothing was run live.** Every number above comes from a rehearsal against fakes. The live run is the lead's, with the owner's go-ahead of 2026-09-30.
- **Deviations:**

Hindsight's own LLM use (retain extraction, consolidation, mental-model refreshes after the template import, reflect) is on the Hindsight side. It is outside the 40; after a live `sec` part the report adds the bank's `llm_request_stats`.

## 2026-09-30: First live triage audit (ticket 33, LIVE)

The corpus was a capped live-verify SEC run (`--only sec --companies lumentum,coherent --limit 2 --keep-db`, live-runs `20260930T095718Z-verify`): live filings, with triage v2 (windows of 1,500 characters with a 200-character overlap, at most 6 per section) deciding 54 sections, 12 retain and 42 skip. The audit ran `atlas triage audit --sample 40 --seed 1 --wait` against that database and its archive, with the full-section judge on MiniMax-M3 via LiteLLM. Audit `0df357b0-5945-411a-94cd-e68f94ba3e72`.

- **Miss rate 1/40 = 2.5%** (Wilson 95% interval 0.4% to 12.9%); population-weighted 2.4%. All 40 samples were judged, none left unjudged.
- **By length band:** under 2k characters 0/24, 2k–10k 0/13, 10k–50k **1/3**. **By method:** role 1/29, rule 0/11. **By form:** 10-K 0/35, 8-K 0/3, EX-99.1 1/2.
- **The one miss:** a Lumentum Q4 FY26 EX-99.1 chunk (11,948 characters, anchor `chunk-002`). Triage read 6 of its 10 windows and called it a "standard GAAP income statement". The judge found a footnote disclosing a **$7.8B non-cash loss on debt extinguishment from convertible-note equitization**, a material financing event (category `dilution_financing`), in the part triage didn't read.
- **Reading:** triage misses little on short sections. Its one miss is the case the windows cap predicts: a long section, with the detail in the unread windows. The sample of long sections is small (3), so any change needs a larger audit of long sections first. The cheap candidate is raising `ATLAS_TRIAGE_WINDOWS_PER_SECTION` (6 → 10 reads every section up to ~13,000 characters in full).
- **Setup notes for running on a live-verify database:** the run's `ATLAS_HINDSIGHT_BANK_ID` must be the live run's bank, whose template application is recorded in that database. Set `ATLAS_HINDSIGHT_VERSION=0.10.1` (the cluster route exposes only `/v1`), and `ATLAS_MENTAL_MODEL_REFRESH_AT=` empty so the worker schedules no refreshes against the deleted bank.

## 2026-09-30: Second live triage audit, a census (ticket 33, LIVE)

The corpus was a live-verify SEC run of Lumentum, Coherent, AXT and Applied Optoelectronics, 2 filings each (`20260930T105350Z-verify`). Triage decided 75 sections: 18 retain and 57 skip. Of the 16 sections longer than 10,000 characters, it retained 13 and skipped 3. The audit was a census of all 57 skipped sections (`--sample 57 --seed 2`), audit `a96e8d01-1527-4875-b08a-2fff7c9c627b`.

- **Miss rate 2/57 = 3.5%** (Wilson 95% interval 1.0% to 11.9%). By length: under 2k characters 0/36, 2k–10k 2/18, 10k–50k 0/3. By form: 10-K 0/37, 10-Q 1/9, 8-K 1/9, EX-99.1 0/2.
- **Miss (a):** a 10-Q "Item 6. Exhibits" section, skipped by the deterministic boilerplate rule. Its exhibit index named "Master Development and Supply Agreement with Coherent Corp" (supplier_customer), so the rule, not the windows, caused it.
- **Miss (b):** an 8-K on buying a 153,928 sq ft Houston property for office, warehouse, light manufacturing and assembly. The role read both windows and called it real estate; the judge called it a capacity addition. This is a rubric judgement.
- **Together with audit 1:** 3 misses in 97 skipped sections (about 3%). Only one of the three (audit 1's EX-99.1 table) came from the window cap; one came from a boilerplate rule and one from the rubric. Tuning follows, in "Triage tuning from the first live audits".

## 2026-09-30: Research roles follow the supply-chain bottleneck method

- **Built** (`docs/decisions.md`, "Research roles follow the supply-chain bottleneck method"; owner direction 2026-09-30; the method of the serenity-skills alignment note, M2, M5, M6, M8, M10, M11, in Atlas's own words, nothing copied from that repo): new prompt versions with unchanged schemas. Scout `scout.v2` (trace demand → hardware → components → sub-components → feedstock/process equipment; who chokes each layer: sole sources, capacity, lead times, qualification, InP/GaAs substrates, indium, gallium, germanium, MOCVD tools, export controls; what relieves it; no generic company-news queries). Investigator `investigator.v2` (priorities: who supplies whom at any layer, single/second sourcing, capacity/allocation/lead times, qualification and design wins, feedstock and equipment dependencies; whitelist predicates and exact quotes unchanged; no layer conflation; absence is not exclusivity). Skeptic `skeptic-plan.v2`, `skeptic.v2` (each checklist item spelled out: substitutes incl. technology transitions EML/VCSEL/SiPh, CPO/LPO; capacity additions incl. new entrants; inventory cycle; customer concentration; dilution/ATM as a standing falsifier). Editor `editor.v3`, `editor-hypothesis.v3` (bottleneck layer, chokepoint and mechanism; `possible_constraint` as "<layer>: <chokepoint> - <mechanism>"; falsifiers always include capacity relief and dilution/financing). The photonics theme's `description` now states the bottleneck hunt. Financial Analyst unchanged.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/roles/prompts/{scout.v2,investigator.v2,skeptic-plan.v2,skeptic.v2,editor.v3,editor-hypothesis.v3}.md` (new), `backend/atlas/roles/{scout,investigator,skeptic,editor}.py` (version constants; `SCOUT_PROMPT_VERSION` added), `configs/themes/ai-infrastructure.yaml`, `tests/unit/test_bottleneck_prompts.py` (new), `tests/integration/{test_discovery,test_claims,test_hypotheses,test_investigations}.py` (new prompt versions / extractor versions), `docs/decisions.md`.
- **Tests:** `tests/unit` (all): 511 passed, including the new structural check (8 cases: each role's prompt name, version and key phrases, whitespace-normalised; both Skeptic prompts name all six checklist items). `tests/integration/{test_discovery,test_claims}.py`: 30 passed; `tests/integration/{test_investigations,test_hypotheses}.py`: 32 passed. ruff format/check clean on `backend` and `tests`; strict pyright clean.
- **Fixture-only vs live:** nothing live. The model answers in every test are scripted, so the tests show the new prompts are loaded, sent and recorded, not that MiniMax follows them. No live run of the new prompts has been made.
- **Deviations:** no schema change (the bottleneck layer, chokepoint and mechanism go in the existing thesis, `mechanism.possible_constraint`, findings order and open questions); the Skeptic's checklist cues are unchanged (technology-transition terms alone don't select a passage yet).
- **Next:** a live extraction smoke run and an `atlas evaluate --live` pass with the new prompts (owner's go-ahead; spends MiniMax quota) to compare against the v1/v2 prompts; then decide whether to widen the `substitutes` cues (silicon photonics, VCSEL, co-packaged optics) after measuring which passages it would add.

## 2026-09-30: pilot fix 01, the document budget split and the card with no finding

- **Built** (`docs/decisions.md`, "The document budget split and the card with no finding (pilot fix 01)"): an Investigator task choosing its documents holds back an equal share of what is left of `max_documents` for each of the round's other Investigator tasks that hasn't chosen yet (`_document_share`: with `L` left and `k` waiting, it may take `L − k·⌊L/(k+1)⌋`; 2 seeds and 25 documents split 13/12), and records `documents` and `documents_dropped` in its artifacts as it chooses, so an unused share passes on. With no accepted Claim the Editor is still called (prompt `editor.v4`, `EDITOR_PROMPT_VERSION = 4`; the request gains `queries` and `read`) and writes a card with `findings: []` and open questions; the stop stays `no_new_independent_evidence`. Every card carries `searched` (each round's queries, leads found and taken) and `read` (per Investigator: documents with the section anchors sent, passages, documents dropped, Claims proposed, accepted and rejected by reason code, or why it read nothing), written by code (`atlas.investigations.coverage`). Saving a Hypothesis needs a card with at least one finding (409 otherwise). The workbench shows "What was searched" and "What was read", and the Save button only when the card has a finding.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/investigations/{coverage.py (new),tasks.py,model.py,service.py}`, `backend/atlas/roles/editor.py`, `backend/atlas/roles/prompts/editor.v4.md` (new), `backend/atlas/hypotheses/service.py`, `frontend/app/investigation/page.tsx`, `frontend/lib/{workbench.ts,api/client.ts,api/openapi.json,api/schema.ts}`, `frontend/unit/workbench.test.ts`, `frontend/e2e/workbench.spec.ts`, `tests/integration/{test_investigations,test_hypotheses,test_scenarios}.py`, `tests/unit/test_bottleneck_prompts.py`, `AGENTS.md`, `docs/decisions.md`.
- **Tests** (the implementer's local results, before the lead's runner CI): new in `tests/integration/test_investigations.py`: the split (2 seeds × 14 notes, budget 25 → `[(12, 2), (13, 1)]` documents taken and dropped; `[(11, 3), (14, 0)]` without the fix) and the card with no finding (a rejected Claim, what the Editor was sent, `searched` and `read`, the save refused with 409, a follow-up round accepted). `tests/integration/{test_investigations,test_hypotheses,test_scenarios,test_investigation_follow_up,test_evaluations}.py`: 49 passed. `tests/unit/test_bottleneck_prompts.py`: 8 passed. Frontend lint, typecheck, unit tests (27 passed) and build clean; ruff and strict pyright clean.
- **Fixture-only vs live:** nothing live. LiteLLM is the scripted fake and Hindsight the recorded fake; whether the Editor's open questions are useful on a real empty round is for the pilot re-run to show.
- **Not run locally:** the Playwright e2e (`scripts/e2e.py`). The machine's `/mnt/c` mount failed as it started; the change was committed afterwards from the intact worktree and the e2e left to the runner CI.
- **Deviations:** an unused share passes on in the order the worker takes the Investigator jobs (the queue orders ties at random), which is plan order only when the worker takes them so; a share is never passed back to a task that already chose. The new split test needs the real clock to be after 2026-09-14 (its imported notes are dated September 2026).
- **Next:** the lead's CI run; re-run pilot investigation 1 once fixes 01 to 05 are released.
## 2026-09-30: pilot fix 02, discovery lead quality (English searches, relevance ranking)

- **Built** (`docs/decisions.md`, "Lead ranking and English searches"): every SearXNG search sends `language` (`ATLAS_SEARXNG_LANGUAGE`, default `en`, validated as a language code: owner direction, English everywhere). `atlas.discovery.ranking` scores each lead against its query and purpose (query terms in title/snippet, purpose terms, universe company names, the config's product and layer terms), multiplies demoted hosts and non-English results by 0.25, and never keeps denied hosts or scores under `min_score`; config `configs/discovery/lead-ranking.yaml` (`ATLAS_LEAD_RANKING_CONFIG`, version 1). The Scout task keeps the top-ranked leads up to the budget, recording each one's `score`, `reasons`, best `query` and `ranking_version` (migration `0042`, down_revision `0040`); `GET /api/v1/investigations/{id}` shows them in `leads`; the task's artifacts add `leads_rejected` and `ranking_version`.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/discovery/ranking.py` (new), `backend/atlas/discovery/searxng.py`, `backend/atlas/settings.py`, `backend/atlas/investigations/{tasks,model}.py`, `backend/atlas/db/migrations/versions/0042_investigation_lead_scores.py` (new), `configs/discovery/lead-ranking.yaml` (new), `tests/fixtures/searxng/coherent-eml-capacity.json` (new, hand-written: pilot 1's query with dictionary, encyclopedia, Swedish broker and translation pages found before two on-topic articles), `tests/unit/test_lead_ranking.py` (new), `tests/unit/test_searxng.py`, `tests/integration/{test_investigations,test_discovery,test_migrations}.py`, `frontend/lib/api/{openapi.json,schema.ts}`, `.env.example`, `AGENTS.md`, `docs/decisions.md`.
- **Tests:** `tests/unit/{test_lead_ranking,test_searxng}.py`: 39 passed (the language sent and validated; the pilot's dictionary, Cambridge, Wikipedia, Avanza and bab.la pages rank below an on-topic article and aren't kept; a Swedish result ranks below the same result in English; English detection; ranking by best sighting). New integration test at the investigation seam (`test_the_scout_keeps_the_top_ranked_leads_with_their_scores_and_reasons`): 7 leads found, the two on-topic ones (found last) kept best first with score and reasons, 5 rejected, `language=en` sent. Updated: the full-flow and budget tests now expect ranked order (AXT, blog, Sumitomo; the Sumitomo article names its query's terms only in the snippet), the discovery test the `language` parameter, the migration head `0042`. Run: `tests/integration/{test_investigations,test_discovery,test_migrations}.py` plus `tests/unit` (all): 569 passed. ruff format/check and strict pyright clean.
- **Fixture-only vs live:** nothing live; SearXNG is the scripted fake over hand-written fixtures. Whether ranking keeps good leads for real queries is for the next pilot investigation to show.
- **Deviations:** leads below `min_score` are never kept, not only ranked lower (a thin discovery can now keep fewer than the budget, even none). English detection is a word-list heuristic (non-ASCII letters, sv/de/nl/fr/es/it function words), not a language model. The workbench page doesn't show the score yet (API only).
- **Next:** the lead's CI run; show score and reasons on the investigation page; tune `min_score` and the host lists from the next pilot investigation's leads.

Also new: prompts `investigator.v3` and `reviewer.v2`; CONTEXT.md "Bottleneck Predicate"; a `docs/decisions.md` entry; the report `docs/research/investigator-yield.md`.

  - their cues fire, and plain product or demand statements and list fragments give none;
  - the v3 and reviewer v2 prompt checks;

  The replay's `--rehearse` mode ran green (v2, relaxed and v3). Ruff (backend, tests, scripts) and strict pyright are clean.

  - With the 9-predicate whitelist:
    - v2: 5 proposed, 3 accepted.
    - relaxed: 9 proposed, 0 accepted (7 `party_not_in_quote`, list fragments).
    - self-statements: 17 proposed, 4 accepted (sole-source and in-house statements rejected `missing_object` or `no_directional_language`; 1 batch quarantined).
  - With the 13-predicate whitelist:
    - **v3: 14 proposed, 8 accepted**, 3 of them bottleneck predicates (`sole_sources` 1, `vertically_integrates` 2); 42.5k tokens in, 4.0k out, 2 repairs.
    - v2 control: 7 proposed, 4 accepted.

  Details and examples are in the report. The Reviewer (v2) was **not** run live; relationship review of the new predicates is fixture-tested only.

## 2026-09-30: Pilot fix 04, page artifacts out of parsed filings (`text-v3`)

- **Built** (`docs/decisions.md`, "Page artifacts and parser version `text-v3`"): parser version `text-v3`. In paginated HTML (a visible block element with `page-break-before/after: always` or `break-before/after: page`; the recorded filings use `<hr style="page-break-after:always"/>`) it removes the "Table of Contents" back-link that opens a page (only a same-document link alone on the page's first line) and the page number that ends a page (1 to 3 digits, and only when it counts up with the pages), then joins a paragraph the page break split (the first part does not end a sentence, the second starts lowercase, neither is a table row or list marker). HTML without page breaks, plain text, PDF and TradingView transcripts parse as before. Archived raw bytes are untouched; existing Source Versions keep their `text-v2` parse; no migration.
- **Left in the text on purpose:** contents headings and the contents page's page references, back-links not alone on a page's first line, lone or out-of-sequence numbers, other page-number forms ("F-12", "Page 3", roman numerals), running titles (they are the statements' own headings in the recorded filings), a split whose second part starts with a capital or digit (1 of 18 in the Coherent 10-K: "… of Applied" | "Materials, Inc. …"), and PDF headers and footers.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/parsing.py`, `tests/unit/test_page_artifacts.py` (new), `tests/integration/test_parser_versions.py` (new), `tests/fixtures/parser/golden.json` (a `text-v3` block; earlier blocks kept), `tests/unit/{test_parsing,test_pdf_parsing}.py`, `tests/integration/{test_ingest,test_assertions,test_pdf_sources}.py` and `frontend/e2e/{assertions,source-viewer}.spec.ts` (the version string and the golden key), `AGENTS.md`, `docs/decisions.md`, `docs/data-model.md`, the ticket.
- **Golden hashes:** two changed, both the intended artifact removal and nothing else (every changed line of all recorded HTML fixtures was diffed against the v2 parse): Lumentum's 10-Q `lite-20260328.htm` (5 back-links, 6 page numbers) and 10-K `lite-20260627.htm` (10 back-links, 41 page numbers). The 8-K and the EX-99.1 keep their v2 hashes (page breaks, no artifacts), so the EX-99.1 Assertion offsets in `test_assertions.py` and the e2e spec are unchanged. The PDF hash in `test_pdf_parsing.py` is unchanged. Coherent's fixtures have no pinned hash; their changes (10-K: 38 back-links, 45 page numbers, 17 joins; 10-Q: 4 and 2) were checked the same way. 40 of the repo's 45 HTML fixtures parse byte-identically to v2; the sectioner's anchors are the same for all six recorded filings.
- **Tests:** red first (`tests/unit/test_page_artifacts.py` failed on the version), then green. `tests/unit` (all): 527 passed (16 new: fragments shaped like Coherent's 10-K pages 8 to 10 and 13|14, Lumentum's 10-Q statement pages and 10-K pages 41|42 and contents page; the recorded Coherent 10-K and both Lumentum filings; what stays: contents heading and references, an out-of-sequence or lone number, a back-link mid-page or to another document, an unpaginated document, a hidden page break, `page-break-after:avoid`; `page-break-before` and `break-after:page`). Integration, one module at a time: `test_parser_versions.py` 2 passed (new: a new version is `text-v3`, its raw bytes are served unchanged and a quote across the former page break is accepted as an Assertion; a version recorded under `text-v2` keeps its parse, hash and Assertion span when the same bytes are imported again, and its revision is parsed by `text-v3`), `test_ingest.py` 22, `test_assertions.py` 46, `test_pdf_sources.py` 7, `test_retention.py` 15, `test_evidence_families.py` 4, `test_claims.py` 15, all passed. `ruff format --check`, `ruff check` (backend, tests, scripts) and strict pyright clean.
- **Not run:** the full integration suite, the e2e tests and the frontend gates (the environment addendum; the two e2e specs were edited by hand: the golden key and the "Parser version" field). The lead's CI run covers them. Other integration modules read the 10-K and 10-Q parses too (investigations, relationships, hypotheses, triage); none holds a literal offset or a "Table of Contents" string, but they were not run here.
- **Fixture-only vs live:** nothing live. The rules were written from the six recorded Coherent and Lumentum filings, which one filing agent's software produced; filings with other markup around page breaks keep their artifacts until their shapes are recorded. Whether the Investigator's yield on the Coherent 10-K improves is for a live run to measure, and only on a version parsed by `text-v3`.
- **Deviations:** no re-parse job (not trivial: a `source_parse` table, a job and every reader of the parse; the design is in the decision entry). A paragraph split by a page break is joined even when the break left no page number or back-link: the ticket asks that the sentence come out whole, and the split itself is the artifact.
- **Open:** the pilot database's already-ingested filings (the Coherent FY2026 10-K among them) stay on their `text-v2` parse, so the pilot's unlocatable quotes stay unlocatable there until the owner chooses a fresh ingest or the re-parse record. A v2 and a v3 parse of the same short document can differ by more than the Evidence Family SimHash threshold (6 for Lumentum's 10-Q fixture, threshold 3).
- **Next:** the lead's CI run; the owner's decision on re-parsing the pilot's filings; record page-break shapes from other filing agents before widening the rules.

## 2026-09-30: pilot fix 05, counterparty companies

- **Built** (`docs/decisions.md`, "Counterparty companies"; CONTEXT.md "Counterparty"; an owner-authorized domain-model extension): a company has a role, `researched` or `counterparty` (migration `0045`, down_revision `0043`; `company.country` nullable for a counterparty; `company.counterparty_resolution`). The Investigator (`investigator.v4`, `INVESTIGATOR_PROMPT_VERSION = 4`) may give a company object as `object_name`, the name as the quote writes it, instead of `object_company_id`. Extraction resolves it (`atlas.counterparties`): first the companies Atlas has, then `resolve_mention` by name (SEC's ticker file and submissions, then GLEIF), exact normalized name and exactly one entity; no match is `unresolved_company`, several `ambiguous_company`, and without `ATLAS_SEC_USER_AGENT` nothing resolves. The counterparty is created only when the Claim passed every check, in the Claim's transaction before its Assertion (audited `company.counterparty_created`). A counterparty is refused by `atlas ingest`, the `ingest` job and as an investigation seed (422); the theme map lists the edges to counterparties and the counterparties themselves (`counterparties`); `GET /api/v1/companies?role=` filters. Promotion is the owner's: adding the company to the theme config, or committing a Candidate with the same CIK or LEI, makes the same row `researched` (audited `company.promoted`).
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/counterparties.py` (new), `backend/atlas/db/migrations/versions/0045_counterparty_companies.py` (new), `backend/atlas/roles/prompts/investigator.v4.md` (new), `backend/atlas/roles/investigator.py`, `backend/atlas/claims/{extraction,handlers}.py`, `backend/atlas/companies.py`, `backend/atlas/identity/service.py`, `backend/atlas/candidates/{proposals,service}.py`, `backend/atlas/investigations/{service,tasks}.py`, `backend/atlas/ledger/ingest.py`, `backend/atlas/themes.py`, `backend/atlas/api/sources.py`, `backend/atlas/cli.py`, `backend/atlas/evaluation/stubs.py`, `frontend/app/{company,theme}/page.tsx`, `frontend/lib/api/{openapi.json,schema.ts}`, `scripts/{e2e,e2e_hypothesis,investigator_replay}.py`, `tests/integration/test_counterparties.py` (new), the scripted Investigator replies in `tests/integration/{test_claims,test_hypotheses,test_investigation_follow_up,test_investigations,test_proposed_updates,test_relationships,test_scenarios}.py` and `tests/live/{test_extraction_smoke_live,verify}.py` (each sends `object_name`), `tests/integration/test_migrations.py` (head `0045`), `tests/unit/test_bottleneck_prompts.py`, `tests/fixtures/identity/` (hand-written: the NVDA ticker row and `CIK0001045810` submissions, four synthetic GLEIF records and their queries, the manifest), `AGENTS.md`, `CONTEXT.md`, `docs/{decisions,data-model}.md`.
- **Tests:** `tests/integration/test_counterparties.py`: 7 passed (Coherent's supply-agreement quote naming NVIDIA becomes a counterparty, an accepted Assertion and a Relationship; a later Claim reuses the counterparty; only a name exactly one registry entity answers to resolves, and only a quote that passes creates one; without entity resolution a named company is unresolved and never created; a counterparty is never ingested and never a seed; adding it to the theme config, or committing its Candidate, promotes the same row). `tests/unit` (all): 542 passed. `tests/integration/{test_claims,test_relationships,test_migrations,test_candidates,test_theme_explorer,test_identity}.py`: 63 passed. ruff format/check (backend, tests, scripts) and strict pyright clean.
- **Fixture-only vs live:** nothing live. SEC and GLEIF are the identity fake over hand-written fixtures (NVIDIA's row from its well-known CIK; the GLEIF records are synthetic, with made-up LEIs), and the Investigator's answers are scripted. Whether MiniMax uses `object_name` correctly, and how real SEC and GLEIF names resolve, is for the pilot re-run to show.
- **Not run locally:** the other integration modules whose scripted replies gained `object_name` (investigations, hypotheses, the follow-up, proposed updates, scenarios), the frontend gates and the e2e: left to the lead's runner CI. The implementing agent was cut off three times by API overloads after the code and tests were written; the lead ran the checks above, wrote this entry and committed.
- **Deviations from the ticket:** only the Investigator creates counterparties (the Reviewer proposes no company). Promotion goes through the theme config or a Candidate's commit, with no separate endpoint. Resolution is exact-name only, so a short form of a legal name ("Sumitomo Electric") stays unresolved.
- **Next:** the pilot re-run; aliases for a counterparty's other spellings; `object_name` in the evaluation gold format; a named subject outside the known companies.

## 2026-09-30: pilot fix 09, Claim precision

- **Built** (`docs/decisions.md`, "Claim precision: the layer from the quote, one clause"): the deterministic **cue-proximity check** `atlas.claims.object_clause_cue`: for a product object, the predicate's cue must be in a clause of the quote that names the object. Clauses are cut only at `;`, `, while`/`whilst`/`whereas`/`but`/`although`/`though` (plus an optional "also"), `, and also`, ` whereas`, ` while also`, never at a bare comma. It applies at extraction (`cue_in_other_clause`) and at review eligibility (`not_eligible` `cue_in_other_clause`, so a researcher's manual Assertion forms no edge either). For the four bottleneck predicates, the object must be in the quote (`object_not_in_quote`) and not only generic words (`generic_object`, whose reason names the tagged layer). `investigator.v5` (layer from the quote's named object, no generic materials/components Claims, named inputs for the bottleneck predicates, "One clause") and `reviewer.v3` (both failure shapes with the pilot's sentences). Schemas unchanged; no migration.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/claims/{predicates,extraction,__init__}.py`, `backend/atlas/relationships/{review,checks}.py`, `backend/atlas/roles/{investigator,reviewer}.py`, `backend/atlas/roles/prompts/{investigator.v5,reviewer.v3}.md` (new), `tests/unit/{test_claim_predicates,test_relationship_checks,test_bottleneck_prompts}.py`, `tests/integration/{test_claims,test_relationships,test_counterparties}.py` (`investigator.v5`), `CONTEXT.md` (Bottleneck Predicate), `docs/decisions.md`, the ticket.
- **Tests (actual results):** `tests/unit/test_claim_predicates.py`, `test_relationship_checks.py` and `test_bottleneck_prompts.py`: 98 passed (new: the VCSEL sentence's two clauses; no cue for three spellings of the VCSEL object; cues found in the object's clause for 8 sentences, 6 of them real sentences from the recorded Coherent and Lumentum 10-Ks with commas inside the clause; each boundary kind; the fallback for a quote not naming its object; generic and specific objects; `names_object`; the reviewer v3 phrases). `tests/integration/test_claims.py`: 16 passed (new: at the extraction seam, the two Lumentum generic sentences tagged `substrate` are rejected `generic_object` with `'substrate'` in the reason, "We purchase InP substrates from a limited number of suppliers" is accepted with layer `substrate`, a paraphrased object is `object_not_in_quote`, and the VCSEL sentence is `cue_in_other_clause` for the VCSEL facilities and accepted for InP capacity with cue "expand"). `tests/integration/test_relationships.py`: 13 passed (new: manual Assertions on the recorded Coherent 10-K's VCSEL sentence: the VCSEL object is `not_eligible` `cue_in_other_clause`, the InP one becomes the only edge). `tests/unit` (all): 585 passed. `scripts/investigator_replay.py --rehearse --variant v5`: runs (4 calls, 16 proposed, 12 accepted, 4 `quote_mismatch`, the scripted fake's deliberate misses). `ruff format --check`, `ruff check` (backend, tests, scripts) and strict pyright clean.
- **Not run:** `tests/integration/test_counterparties.py` (only its version string changed), other integration modules, the e2e and the frontend gates (the environment addendum); the lead's runner CI covers them.
- **Fixture-only vs live:** nothing live. Whether MiniMax follows `investigator.v5` and `reviewer.v3` is unmeasured; the replay harness's live mode or the next pilot re-run can measure it with the owner's go-ahead.
- **Deviations:** the generic-object and object-in-quote rules are deterministic only for the four bottleneck predicates (the prompt states them for all); the older product predicates keep their behaviour, so the e2e's and replay's scripted `manufactures` Claims are unaffected. A wrong but specific layer (the Industrial segment's "exotic materials, crystals, and optics" tagged `substrate`) is left to the Reviewer, since no rule can tell a layer from words reliably. Company objects are not checked for clause proximity. Existing production Assertions are not re-judged (the VCSEL edge stays for the owner to reject).
- **Next:** a live replay of v5 against v4; a live `review_relationships` over the pilot's accepted Assertions with `reviewer.v3`.
## 2026-09-30: pilot fix 10, the passage budget spread over the documents

- **Cause** (ticket, "Why only the 10-K"): the extraction kept the first 24 of *all documents' entity-tagged windows, then all recall-only windows*, in document order. The newest long filing (the 10-K) has dozens of tagged windows and the recall hits, so it filled the budget; press releases and 10-Qs, which rarely name another universe company, never got a passage.
- **Built** (`docs/decisions.md`, "The passage budget is spread over the documents (pilot fix 10)"): `ATLAS_INVESTIGATION_MAX_PASSAGES` (default 24), the Investigator task's passage budget (the `extract_claims` job and the Skeptic keep `investigator_max_passages`). `_select` deals passages one per document per round in the documents' order (newest first in an investigation), so each document gets an equal share, the remainder goes to the earlier ones and an unusable share passes on. Within a document, tagged windows come first, then recall-only ones. A document with neither is read from its lead windows (`selected_by: ["lead"]`; results sections first: 10-Q/10-K MD&A, 8-K Items 2.02/7.01/8.01; the cover last). Also: `passages_by_document` in the extraction's and the Investigator task's artifacts, `passages` per document on the card's `read` (`CardDocumentRead.passages`, default 0), and the count on the investigation page. Batches, offsets, checks and continuation are unchanged; the Investigator and Editor prompts and requests are unchanged; no migration.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/claims/{extraction,handlers,reads}.py`, `backend/atlas/investigations/{tasks,coverage,model}.py`, `backend/atlas/settings.py`, `frontend/app/investigation/page.tsx`, `frontend/lib/api/{openapi.json,schema.ts}` (regenerated), `tests/integration/{test_claims,test_investigations}.py`, `AGENTS.md`, `docs/decisions.md`, the ticket.
- **Measured on the recorded fixtures** (every retained section a recall hit under the Hindsight fake): Lumentum 10-K 8, 8-K 3, EX-99.1 8, 10-Q 5 (before: 24 of the 10-K); Coherent 10-K 21, 10-Q 3 (before: 24 and 0). The recorded Lumentum filings name no other universe company; Coherent's 10-K has 7 tagged windows of 88.
- **Tests:** `tests/integration/test_claims.py` 17 passed (2 new: the budget dealt 3/2 over the 10-K and 10-Q in alternation, tagged windows first; Lumentum's EX-99.1 with no tag and no question read whole from its lead windows beside the 10-K's tagged ones). `tests/integration/test_investigations.py` 24 passed (1 new: an investigation seeded with Lumentum reads all four filings, 8/3/8/5, first round newest first, `passages_by_document` in the task's artifacts and per-document `passages` on the card; the budget-exhaustion test now sets `investigation_max_passages`). `tests/unit` 558 passed. `ruff format --check`, `ruff check` (backend, tests, scripts) and strict pyright clean. The API client was regenerated with `scripts/export_openapi.py` and the main checkout's `openapi-typescript` 7.13.0 binary (the worktree has no `node_modules`).
- **Not run locally:** the frontend gates (lint, typecheck, unit) and the e2e (environment addendum), and the other integration modules that run investigations (`test_investigation_follow_up`, `test_hypotheses`, `test_scenarios`, `test_proposed_updates`, `test_counterparties`, the evaluation runner): their Investigators now read a newest-first spread instead of the 10-K's first 24 windows, and a document with no tagged or recalled window now sends lead windows. Left to the lead's runner CI.
- **Fixture-only vs live:** nothing live. The production cause is inferred from the code and the re-run's recorded card sections, not re-measured on production data; whether the press releases' passages yield Claims is for the next pilot run.
- **Deviations:** the lead windows are an addition the ticket implies ("press releases get a share") but doesn't name: a fair share alone cannot read a document that no rule selects. `passages_dropped` counts only tagged and recall-only windows not sent.
- **Next:** the pilot re-run, comparing Claims from EX-99.1 and 10-Q passages with the 10-K's.

## 2026-09-30: pilot-fixes ticket 11, re-parsing recorded Source Versions (`source_parse`)

- **Built** (`docs/decisions.md`, "Re-parsing recorded Source Versions"; fix 04's design items 1–3, item 4 designed only): migration `0046` (down_revision `0045`): the insert-only `source_parse` table keyed by (Source Version, parser version), never the recorded parser version (triggers ENABLE ALWAYS); the view `source_version_parse` (recorded parse + re-parses); `assertion.parser_version` (NOT NULL, backfilled with the recorded parser version, immutable, checked by the insert trigger); `claim.parser_version` (nullable). The `reparse` job (`atlas.ledger.parses`, not pausable, no provider budget) re-parses the archived raw bytes of versions recorded under an earlier parser of `PARSER_LINEAGE`, archives the text and inserts the row, audited `source_parse.created`, idempotent. `atlas ledger reparse [--company] [--parser-version] [--key]`. `GET /api/v1/source-versions/{id}` gains `parses` and `current_parser_version`; `.../content?kind=parsed&parser_version=` serves one parse.
- **Which readers use which parse:** the Investigator's extraction reads the current parse (the `text-v3` re-parse when it has text, else the recorded one); passages, Claims and Assertions record it, and a resumed extraction reads the parse its passages name. Assertion creation (`POST /api/v1/assertions` takes an optional `parser_version`), the Relationship review's verbatim check, the evaluation runner and the source viewer's highlight use the Assertion's own parse; proposed-update "same span" matches also require the same parse; Research Snapshots record each Assertion's `parser_version`. Everything else (retention, triage and its audit, provenance/recall/reflect, the Skeptic, contradiction detection of vanished quotes, replay, Evidence Families) stays on the recorded parse, unchanged when no `source_parse` row exists.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/db/migrations/versions/0046_source_parse.py` (new), `backend/atlas/ledger/parses.py` (new), `backend/atlas/ledger/{reads,__init__}.py`, `backend/atlas/parsing.py` (`PARSER_LINEAGE`), `backend/atlas/assertions.py`, `backend/atlas/claims/{extraction,reads}.py`, `backend/atlas/relationships/review.py`, `backend/atlas/proposed_updates/{triggers,detection}.py`, `backend/atlas/evaluation/runner.py`, `backend/atlas/snapshots.py`, `backend/atlas/jobs/handlers.py`, `backend/atlas/api/sources.py`, `backend/atlas/cli.py`, `frontend/app/version/page.tsx`, `frontend/components/assertions.tsx`, `frontend/lib/api/{client.ts,openapi.json,schema.ts}`, `tests/integration/test_reparse.py` (new), `tests/integration/test_migrations.py` (head `0046`, the backfill), `AGENTS.md`, `docs/{decisions,data-model,runbooks}.md`, the ticket.
- **Tests (actual results, one module at a time):** `test_reparse.py` 5 passed (a `text-v2` version re-parsed to `text-v3`: a separate row with the v3 hash, the recorded parse and hash unchanged, both parses served, the audit event and `atlas audit verify`, a second run inserts nothing; the table refuses UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE and a row under the recorded parser; only the current parser re-parses; an extraction on the re-parsed version accepts a quote across the former page break, its passages, Claim and Assertion record `text-v3`, and an older Assertion still verifies against `text-v2`; an Assertion is checked against the parse it names and its parse is immutable). `test_migrations.py` 5, `test_assertions.py` + `test_parser_versions.py` 48, `test_claims.py` 15, `test_relationships.py` 12, `test_ingest.py` + `test_queue_pause.py` 33, all passed. `tests/unit` 558 passed. `ruff format --check`, `ruff check` (backend, tests, scripts) and strict pyright clean. API client regenerated.
- **Not run:** the full integration suite (notably `test_proposed_updates`, `test_hypotheses`, `test_investigations`, `test_evaluation`, whose SQL changed only by the added parse condition/column), the frontend gates and e2e (no `frontend/node_modules` in the worktree): **the source viewer change is untested locally**. Left to the runner CI.
- **Fixture-only vs live:** nothing live; the document is the hand-shaped Coherent 10-K fragment of `test_parser_versions.py`, the Investigator's answer scripted.
- **Deviations:** the re-parse selects only versions whose recorded parse has text under an earlier version of the same parser (failed first parses and TradingView transcripts are not re-parsed). A re-parse with the same text as the recorded one is still recorded. The Skeptic stays on the recorded parse (the ticket names only the Investigator). No Assertion filter by parse in the list API; the relationship Evidence read doesn't expose `parser_version` (the viewer reads it from the Assertion).
- **Next:** on the pilot database, `atlas ledger reparse` after deploying `0046`, then re-run the Lumentum extraction to recover the `capacity_constrained` quote; the owner decides whether to build the retain-the-re-parse `reprocess` (designed in the decision entry).

## 2026-09-30: pilot fix 06, the Skeptic reads the archive

- **Diagnosis** (ticket, "Resolution"): the pilot's `skeptic-plan.v2` call was sent a full catalog (Coherent's and Lumentum's 10-Ks, 10-Qs, 8-Ks and EX-99.1s) and answered 10 queries and `"documents": []`. It chose from the archive and chose nothing; its search matched no catalog URL, and no code noticed.
- **Built** (`docs/decisions.md`, "The Skeptic reads the archive: a deterministic fallback (pilot fix 06)"): after the Skeptic's search, each seed company that neither the plan nor the search covered gets its latest 10-K and 10-Q (or its latest two primary documents), plus its newest already-read document when none of those was read (free of budget); then the theme's other companies' latest 10-Ks while `max_documents` has room. A follow-up round's fallback leaves out what an earlier round's Skeptic read. Recorded as `selected_by: fallback`, a `skeptic_documents_fallback` event, the task's `documents_fallback`/`documents_from_fallback`, and a reason on the task (`detail`) when the Skeptic read nothing or no passage. Prompt `skeptic-plan.v3` (same schema): why and what to choose from the catalog. The research card's `read` gains a Skeptic row (`role`, `documents_fallback`, each document's `selected_by`; counterevidence counts in the `claims_*`/`rejected` fields), after the round's Investigators; the Editor is still sent the Investigators' rows only. The workbench page shows the row ("Reader" column, "Skeptic", "(chosen by code)"). No migration.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/investigations/{skeptic,coverage,model,tasks}.py`, `backend/atlas/roles/skeptic.py`, `backend/atlas/roles/prompts/skeptic-plan.v3.md` (new), `backend/atlas/evaluation/stubs.py` (the Skeptic's reading defaults to no counterevidence when a case scripts none), `frontend/lib/workbench.ts`, `frontend/app/investigation/page.tsx`, `frontend/unit/workbench.test.ts`, `frontend/e2e/workbench.spec.ts`, `frontend/lib/api/{openapi.json,schema.ts}`, `scripts/e2e.py`, `tests/live/verify.py` (rehearsal answers the reading call), `tests/unit/test_bottleneck_prompts.py`, `tests/integration/{test_investigations,test_investigation_follow_up,test_hypotheses,test_proposed_updates,test_scenarios}.py` (the scripted Skeptic answers both call shapes; one more Skeptic call, token totals and `read` rows where a plan chose nothing), `docs/decisions.md`, the ticket.
- **Tests** (one module at a time): red first (`tests/unit/test_bottleneck_prompts.py` on `skeptic-plan` v3; the new integration test on the missing fallback), then green. New: `test_a_skeptic_plan_that_chooses_no_document_falls_back_to_each_seed_company_s_filings` (the pilot's seeds, Coherent and Lumentum, both ingested from the recorded EDGAR fixtures; the plan answers `"documents": []`; the Skeptic reads Coherent's 10-K and 10-Q and Lumentum's 10-K and 10-Q, passages of at least one of each company's, uncharged; the event, the artifacts, the plan's prompt version 3, and the card's Skeptic row); the no-Claim card test asserts the skipped Skeptic's row and its reason; the follow-up test asserts round 2's row (Lumentum's note chosen, no passage matching the checklist, and the reason). Results: `test_investigations.py` + `test_investigation_follow_up.py` 30 passed; `test_hypotheses.py` 12, `test_proposed_updates.py` 3, `test_scenarios.py` 5, `test_evaluations.py` 4, all passed; `tests/unit` 558 passed; frontend lint, typecheck and unit tests (28) passed; `ruff format`, `ruff check` (backend, tests, scripts) and strict pyright clean; API client regenerated.
- **Not run:** the e2e (`scripts/e2e.py`, `frontend/e2e/workbench.spec.ts` edited by hand for the Skeptic's row), the frontend build and the full integration suite (environment addendum): left to the runner CI. The live verification's rehearsal model was edited but not run.
- **Fixture-only vs live:** nothing live. Whether MiniMax now chooses documents under `skeptic-plan.v3` is unmeasured; the fallback doesn't depend on it. Live, the fallback adds Skeptic reading calls (at most one per investigation in `scripts/live-verify.sh`'s settings, 8 passages per call) and tokens to every investigation whose plan leaves a seed company out.
- **Deviations:** the fallback runs after the Skeptic's search, not right after the plan, so a seed company the search matched is covered (the ticket's design said "when the plan chooses no documents"). "Minimum" is read as one document per seed company that has one. "Other companies in the theme" are the theme config's companies (not the Claims' object companies). A follow-up round's fallback skips what an earlier Skeptic read (not in the brief). The Editor's request is unchanged: it is not sent the Skeptic's row. In the harnesses, the scripted "nothing found" Skeptic answers both call shapes and is queued 8 times; the hypotheses and proposed-updates helpers drop an earlier investigation's unused Skeptic answers.
- **Next:** the lead's CI run (with the e2e); a live pilot re-run to see whether the plan now chooses documents and whether the fallback's reading yields counterevidence.

## 2026-09-30: pilot fix 12, EDGAR full-text search as a discovery channel

- **Built** (`docs/decisions.md`, "EDGAR full-text search as a discovery channel"): `atlas.discovery.edgar_fts`, an EDGAR full-text search client through the SEC HTTP client (User-Agent, shared 10 req/s limiter, retries); the Scout (`scout.v3`) and the Skeptic's plan (`skeptic-plan.v3`) gain a nullable `filing_phrase` per query, searched in 10-K/10-Q/8-K/20-F/6-K/40-F filings of the 18 months before the as-of time; hits become `edgar_fts` leads (title `<filer> <form> filed <date>`, the document's EDGAR archive URL, CIK, form, dates, universe company, archived Source Version, `ingestable`), ranked (v2) against the matched phrase; a discovery fails only when no search of either channel succeeded; `propose_candidates` examines filing leads by CIK without the mention extractor (`lead_examination.method` `filer_cik`) and the investigation Scout enqueues it with `filers_only`; the Skeptic's discovery uses both channels (the SearXNG and EDGAR search loop is now shared, `atlas.discovery.service.run_searches`/`add_query`). Settings `ATLAS_DISCOVERY_EDGAR_FTS` (auto/on/off), `ATLAS_DISCOVERY_EDGAR_MAX_HITS` (10), `ATLAS_SEC_EFTS_URL`. The first failure needed no fix, so the lead re-ran the failed job on `v0.2.4` (the tag was not moved); that re-run found the clock defect. The `pilot-review` skill gains the baseline step and the **Baseline** label. No migration, no API change.
- **Tests (actual results):** `tests/unit/test_archive_baseline.py` 7 passed (written before the module, but first run after it, so no red run was seen). `ruff format`, `ruff check` and strict pyright clean on the three files. The rest of the suite was not run locally.
- **Live (read-only):** one run of the script against production 0.2.3 for investigation 1's 0.2.3 run (`4620f8c2-…`): 145 requests and one recall, no LLM call; 69 documents, 20 hits, 2 containing an accepted Claim's quote, all 20 in sections recall resolved to. ## 2026-10-01: how Hindsight is used, the budgets raised, and the spec for memory-directed reading

- **Asked by the owner:** whether Atlas uses Hindsight to the maximum; to align with the Serenity method; that the MiniMax subscription is dedicated to this project; to adjust what needs adjusting during the dev cycle.
- **The usage map** (read from the code): inside an investigation Hindsight is used twice. The Scout is sent the Bottlenecks mental model's text, the same for every question. Each Investigator makes one recall with the whole question, scoped to its one seed company, budget `mid`; the result is reduced to a set of sections, its ranking discarded. The Skeptic, the Financial Analyst and the Editor use no Memory; reflect, the Theme status model, theme-scoped recall and the recall options are used by no role; nothing Atlas concludes is retained.
- **A recall probe on production** (five `POST /api/v1/memory/recall` calls, no LLM call): the question with theme scope returns generic product facts; the Scout's kind of query with theme scope returns AXT's three-year 6-inch InP substrate supply agreement with Coherent and AXT's export-permit backlog, from filings investigation 1 never opened; a focused query on Lumentum returns the allocation statement. `high` and `mid` budgets returned the same 40 memories.
- **Budgets** (home-ops PR #7174, the owner merges): `ATLAS_MINIMAX_BUDGET_TOKENS` 400,000 → 4,000,000; `ATLAS_RUN_TOKEN_BUDGET` 200,000 → 1,000,000; `ATLAS_INVESTIGATION_MAX_PASSAGES` 24 → 72; `ATLAS_INVESTIGATOR_MAX_PASSAGES` 24 → 48. The Codex budget is unchanged. The pilot's cost and latency bars were changed to match (the criteria ticket), before any run under the new budgets.
- **The spec:** `.scratch/atlas-memory-directed-reading/spec.md` with eight tickets: reading pointers (the Scout's queries asked of Memory across the theme, stored); Claim checks (the filer's impersonal sentences, the typographic fold, constraint and ownership cues); contradiction or bear context; specific filing phrases; passage selection by pointer and search rank; multi-hop Investigators; the Skeptic reading by pointers; a layer only when the quote supports one. Pilot-fix tickets 13–20 are superseded by them. Memory still never reaches a role as a statement and is never a witness.
- **The pilot-review map** was redrawn: this build comes before investigations 2–5 (ticket 14); investigation 1 is re-run on its release (ticket 15); investigations 2–5 also run once on 0.2.5 without review, for breadth (ticket 16).
- **Nothing built yet;** the recall probe was live and read-only.

## 2026-10-01: memory-directed reading ticket 01, reading pointers

- **Built** (`docs/decisions.md`, "Memory as the reading index"; `CONTEXT.md`, **Reading pointer**): once an investigation's Scout has its queries, its task asks Memory the round's question and each query across the theme (one `theme:<slug>` recall each through `atlas.research`, no LLM call, no budget) and stores every Source Version section a resolved memory leads to as a `reading_pointer` row: the query (index and text, its `discovery_query_id`), the recall's rank, the memory's ID, type and text, the Source Version, the section's anchor, heading and offsets, the company, the `available_at` checked against the as-of time, and the citation state. An observation points at each section of its source facts; a version available after `as_of`, and an unverified or broken memory, make no pointer. Insert-only (migration `0051`, down_revision `0050`: triggers refuse UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE), stored in one transaction per task with a `pointers_recorded` event, the task's counts and one audit event (`investigation.pointers_recorded`, actor `atlas-scout`). A recall that fails for its own reason is a `pointer_recall_failed` event and the Scout goes on; a Hindsight quota or outage pauses the task, and the resumed attempt asks again and stores once (the recalls now run before the leads are taken). `GET /api/v1/investigations/{id}` returns `pointers` (with the document's title and the company's name); the Scout task's artifacts count `pointer_recalls`, `pointer_recalls_failed`, `memories_recalled`, `memories_unresolved`, `sections_after_as_of`, `pointers`, `pointers_by_company`; the investigation page has a "Reading pointers" section under the plan, one collapsed table per query, the text labelled Memory, not Evidence; the Research Snapshot carries them (`memory.reading_pointers`). Nothing reads the pointers yet, no prompt changed, and no role is sent Memory.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/investigations/{pointers.py (new),tasks.py,model.py,__init__.py}`, `backend/atlas/db/migrations/versions/0051_reading_pointers.py` (new), `backend/atlas/snapshots.py`, `backend/atlas/api/investigations.py` (docstring), `frontend/app/investigation/page.tsx`, `frontend/lib/{workbench.ts,api/client.ts,api/openapi.json,api/schema.ts}` (the last two regenerated), `frontend/unit/workbench.test.ts`, `tests/fakes/hindsight.py` (`fail_recalls`), `tests/unit/test_hindsight_fake_derivations.py`, `tests/integration/{test_investigations,test_investigation_follow_up,test_hypotheses,test_migrations}.py`, `CONTEXT.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `docs/decisions.md`, the ticket.
- **Tests run (local, under WSL, one module at a time; actual results):** `tests/integration/test_investigations.py` 30 passed (five new: the question and two Scout queries asked across the theme and stored as pointers for the seed Coherent and the non-seed Lumentum, with query, rank, section, offsets and Memory text, an observation pointing at both companies' sections, and none for Lumentum's 10-K, available two days after the as-of time; insert-only and `atlas audit verify` exit 0; a recall answering HTTP 500 on one query leaves the Scout succeeded with the other queries' pointers and a `pointer_recall_failed` event naming it; recalls returning nothing leave no pointer and `pointers_recorded` names the three queries; a 503 on a recall pauses the Scout, which resumes without a second Scout call or search and stores the pointers once. Two existing tests updated: the Scout's event story gains `pointers_recorded`, and the never-a-witness test now counts the Scout's four theme recalls beside the Investigator's and checks no role was sent a pointed memory). The first red run failed as intended (the recalls' scope, then `KeyError: 'pointers'`). `tests/integration/test_investigation_follow_up.py` 6 passed (round 2 keeps its own pointers, its query 0 the open question); `test_hypotheses.py` 12 passed (the snapshot's `memory.reading_pointers` equal the investigation's); `test_migrations.py` 5 passed (head `0051`); `test_proposed_updates.py` 3, `test_scenarios.py` 6, `test_counterparties.py` 7, `test_edgar_discovery.py` 3, `test_retention_triage.py` 14, `test_research.py` 17, `test_evaluations.py` 4 passed; `tests/unit` 614 passed (one new: `fail_recalls`). `ruff format --check`, `ruff check` (backend, tests, scripts) and strict `pyright` (whole project) clean. Frontend: `lint` and `typecheck` clean, `test` 29 passed (one new: pointers grouped by query, best rank first, companies counted; it was written with the function, so no red run was seen). Not run locally: the other integration modules, `npm run build`, the e2e and `scripts/ci.sh`: left to the runners.
- **Fixture vs live:** fixtures only. Hindsight is the recorded fake, which recalls every in-scope memory whatever the query (one derived fact per retained section, plus the test's observation), so the tests show scope, rank, resolution, the as-of rule and the failure paths, not relevance. No recall was made against a real Hindsight for this ticket; whether theme-scoped recalls of real Scout queries point where the spec's probe says is the pilot's to show.
- **Deviations:** (1) the pointers are also written into the Research Snapshot (`memory.reading_pointers`; `memory.used` true when there are any): the spec's "Pointers are stored" decision asks for it, the ticket's bullets don't list it, and no other ticket does. (2) One audit event per task's pointer set (hash over the rows), not one per pointer: a round can store a few hundred rows and the audit chain is appended under a lock. (3) Beyond the ticket's column list the row keeps `task_id`, `discovery_query_id`, `section_heading` and `available_at`; `citation_state` is always `resolved` (a CHECK). (4) A `pointers_recorded` event is written for every Scout task (also with no pointer), so existing event-story assertions gained it. (5) The fake gained `fail_recalls` (a hand-written failure body; listed with the fake's derivations). (6) `scripts/gen_api_client.sh` could not be run as a script here (CRLF checkout under WSL), so its two commands were run by hand (`scripts/export_openapi.py`, then `openapi-typescript`); CI's `--check` is the proof.
- **Next:** ticket 05 (passage selection by pointer), 06 (Investigators for the companies the pointers name), 07 (the Skeptic's recalls stored the same way: `task_id` is there for it; a per-checklist query will need its own query identity beside `query_index`). A round stores up to (queries + 1) × the recall's result size rows, all returned by the investigation read: if the page's polling gets heavy on production, page or summarize `pointers`.
## 2026-10-01: memory-directed reading ticket 02, Claim checks

- **Built** (`docs/decisions.md`, "Claim checks: the filer as the unnamed party, the typographic fold, constraint and ownership cues"; the ticket's "Resolution"):
  - **The filer as the unnamed party.** A Claim whose subject or object is the document's filer is accepted without the filer's name or a first-person word in the quote when the quote names the other company (a company object) or the product (a product object), carries the predicate's cue, and names no other company: none of the companies Atlas has besides the Claim's two, and no name written with a legal form that isn't the named party's (`atlas.claims.stray_companies`). `claim.party_basis` is `filer`, else `named` (accepted Claims; null on rejected ones). A sentence naming two other companies, and the same sentence in another company's document, stay `party_not_in_quote`.
  - **The typographic fold** (`atlas.claims.fold`, `FOLD_TABLE`: 20 characters, one to one): a quote with no exact occurrence is placed through it, at the model's offsets or at its one folded occurrence (`offset_source` `folded`; several are `quote_ambiguous`). The Claim's and the Assertion's quote is the archived text at the span; the cue, party and object checks read it through the same fold and record the cue as written.
  - **`capacity_constrained` cues:** the bare "allocat…" stem is replaced by allocation-of-supply patterns, and "demand is outpacing …" is matched; the capacity vocabulary is generic for `generic_object` and `names_object`.
  - **Direction from the wording** (`atlas.claims.direction_refusal`, rejection `wrong_direction`): `owns` from the holder of issued or purchased shares to their issuer; the company whose purchase commitment it is, is the buyer.
  - **Investigator `investigator.v6`** (v5 kept): the examples the ticket names; the `owns` definition in the request states the direction. Schema unchanged; `party_basis` is derived.
  - **Migration `0052`** (`down_revision` `0050`; the lead re-chains): `claim.party_basis`, and `folded` in the `claim.offset_source` check. Its downgrade refuses while a `folded` Claim exists.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/claims/{predicates,extraction,reads,__init__}.py`, `backend/atlas/roles/investigator.py`, `backend/atlas/roles/prompts/investigator.v6.md` (new), `backend/atlas/db/migrations/versions/0052_claim_party_basis.py` (new), `frontend/lib/api/{openapi.json,schema.ts}` (regenerated: `party_basis`, `folded`), `tests/unit/test_claim_predicates.py`, `tests/unit/test_bottleneck_prompts.py`, `tests/integration/test_claims.py`, `tests/integration/{test_migrations,test_counterparties,test_relationships}.py` (the head revision; `investigator.v6`), `docs/{decisions,data-model,implementation-log}.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `CONTEXT.md`, the ticket.
- **Tests (actual results, under WSL, one module at a time):**
  - `tests/unit/test_claim_predicates.py` 92 passed (25 new cases: the pilot's constraint statements as cues, the expansion and capital-allocation sentences as none, the capacity vocabulary, `owns` in four issuance and purchase sentences both ways, the purchase commitment's buyer, the fold's table, cues read through the fold and returned as written, stray companies). `tests/unit/test_bottleneck_prompts.py` 8 passed (the Investigator at v6 with the new phrases).
  - `tests/integration/test_claims.py` 21 passed (3 new, at the extraction seam through the CLI, a worker pass and the API): the NVIDIA purchase-commitment sentence in the filer's document is an accepted `supplies` Claim with `party_basis` `filer`, `buys_from` for it is `wrong_direction`, a sentence naming two other companies is `party_not_in_quote` for either company as the object and for a product object, and the same sentence in another company's document is rejected for Coherent and accepted for that document's filer; the slide bullet with U+2011 quoted with ASCII hyphens is accepted as `manufactures` at the model's offsets and at wrong ones (`offset_source` `folded`, the Claim's and the Assertion's quote the archived text), a quote differing by a word is `quote_mismatch`, and "6-inch" occurring twice once folded is `quote_ambiguous`; the expansion sentence is `no_directional_language` and a constraint of "manufacturing capacity" is `generic_object`; NVIDIA `owns` the issuer is accepted and the issuer `owns` NVIDIA is `wrong_direction`.

## 2026-10-01: memory-directed reading ticket 03, contradiction or bear context

- **Built** (`docs/decisions.md`, "Contradiction or bear context"): the Skeptic's accepted counterevidence is one of two kinds, never both. Its answer (`skeptic.v3`) gives each item a `kind`, a contradiction's `how` (`denies`, `limits`, `dates`) and a table row's `figure_name` and `figure_period`. Code decides the kind: a contradiction must name a supporting Claim, say how, and its quote must name that Claim's subject company or its object (a company by name, or the Claim's object text as `names_object` reads it; the filer's "we" is not a name; a generic object text names nothing). A proposed contradiction that fails is accepted as bear context with the reason (`kind_reason`). A quote that is a table row with no words (`is_table_row`: a label and at least two figure cells) is rejected `table_row_without_figure` unless the item states the figure's name and period, and is never a contradiction. Independence, the finding's `counterevidence_ids` and `needs_review`, the stop detail, premise disproof and the proposed-update trigger are contradictions' only; bear context records its Evidence Family and no independence. The research card gains `bear_context` (groups by checklist item, in checklist order, and company); `contradictions` holds contradictions only, each with `how`. The Editor (`editor.v5`) is sent `contradictions` and `bear_context` separately (replacing `counterevidence`) and writes open questions from both. The investigation page shows the contradictions table, a count by kind, and a Bear context section on the card. Glossary: Counterevidence, Bear Context.
- **Migration `0053_counterevidence_kind`** (`down_revision` `0050`, to be re-chained): `kind` (NOT NULL), `how`, `kind_reason`, `figure_name`, `figure_period` on `counterevidence`. Rows recorded before it are backfilled `contradiction` when `contradicts_claim_ids` is not empty, else `bear_context`; nothing else about them changes (a backfilled bear-context row keeps its recorded `independent`). The insert-only table's no-update trigger is disabled for the one backfill statement and re-enabled (ENABLE ALWAYS). The unnamed check "accepted = `independent` set" (found by its definition) is replaced by `counterevidence_independence` (rejected: none; accepted contradiction: set; bear context: either), with `counterevidence_kind_check`, `counterevidence_kind_names_claim` (an accepted row is a contradiction exactly when it names a Claim), `counterevidence_how_of_contradiction` and `counterevidence_kind_reason_of_bear_context`. The downgrade refuses while an accepted row has no `independent`.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/db/migrations/versions/0053_counterevidence_kind.py` (new), `backend/atlas/roles/prompts/{skeptic.v3,editor.v5}.md` (new), `backend/atlas/roles/{skeptic,editor}.py`, `backend/atlas/investigations/{skeptic,model,tasks,coverage,service}.py`, `backend/atlas/proposed_updates/{triggers,detection}.py` (only a contradiction is an event), `backend/atlas/evaluation/{stubs,runner}.py` (the scripted Skeptic answers the new fields; the observed counterevidence carries its `kind`), `backend/atlas/api/investigations.py` and `backend/atlas/hypotheses/drafting.py` (docstrings), `frontend/app/investigation/page.tsx`, `frontend/lib/workbench.ts`, `frontend/unit/workbench.test.ts`, `frontend/lib/api/{openapi.json,schema.ts}`, `frontend/e2e/hypothesis-dossier.spec.ts`, `scripts/e2e_hypothesis.py`, `tests/unit/test_skeptic_checks.py` (new), `tests/unit/test_bottleneck_prompts.py`, `tests/integration/{test_investigations,test_hypotheses,test_proposed_updates,test_migrations}.py`, `CONTEXT.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `docs/decisions.md`, the ticket.
- **Tests** (WSL, one module at a time; real Postgres):
  - New, at the investigation seam (`test_investigations.py`): `test_one_contradiction_marks_one_finding_and_the_rest_is_bear_context_on_the_card` (Coherent seeded, Lumentum ingested; the Skeptic's plan chooses a later statement limiting the supply agreement, Coherent's recorded 10-K and 10-Q and Lumentum's recorded 10-K; its answer has the limiting quote as a contradiction, the 10-K's customer-concentration sentence and the 10-Q's "Inventories" row with its figure as bear context, and Lumentum's "for certain components we have sole or limited source supply arrangements" proposed as a contradiction of Coherent's Claim: one finding contradicted, three bear-context items in three groups on the card, the Lumentum item stored as bear context with the reason, stop `needs_review` "1 findings are contradicted by independent counterevidence", the Editor sent the two lists, prompts `skeptic` 3 and `editor` 5) and `test_a_table_row_is_bear_context_only_with_its_figure_s_name_and_period` (two rows without a name and period rejected `table_row_without_figure`; a row naming Coherent, proposed as a contradiction with its figure, stored as bear context).

Left to the runner CI.

## 2026-10-01: memory-directed reading, ticket 04: specific filing phrases; a filer needs a kept hit

- **Built** (`docs/decisions.md`, "Specific filing phrases; a filer needs a kept hit"):
  - **The phrase rule.** A filing phrase is searched in EDGAR only when it has at least two words (a hyphen divides words) or is a product or layer term of the lead-ranking config (`terms`); of several quoted phrases one such is enough. `atlas.discovery.edgar_fts.filing_phrase_skip_reason`, held by the client (`EdgarFullTextSearch.skip_reason`; `from_settings` loads the config's terms), so `add_query` applies it for the Scout's and the Skeptic's discoveries with no change at their call sites. A skipped search is an `edgar_search` row with status `skipped` and `skip_reason`, never sent; `run_searches` counts it (`filing_skipped`), the `discover` job's and the Scout task's artifacts have `filing_searches_skipped`, and `GET /api/v1/discoveries/{id}` shows it.
  - **Migration `0054`** (`down_revision` `0050`): `edgar_search.status` gains `skipped`, the column `skip_reason`, and the checks that a skipped search has a reason and no `searched_at`. Needed: 0050's status check refuses `skipped`. Its downgrade refuses while a skipped search exists.
  - **Lead ranking version 3.** A filing hit is scored against the query that found it and its purpose, like a web lead (version 2: against the phrase EDGAR matched, so every hit was kept). A hit whose search matched one phrase that is itself a vocabulary term is multiplied by the new `bare_term_factor` (0.25; `Sighting.matched`, `score_lead(matched=)`). The config's bottleneck vocabulary is its own list, `method_terms` (scored as before; not a product or layer for the phrase rule). `atlas.discovery.leads.discovery_sightings` builds a discovery's sightings for ranking, used by the investigation's lead choice and by the proposer. A web lead's score is unchanged.
  - **Candidates.** `propose_candidates` proposes a filer outside the universe only when one of its filing leads of the discovery is kept by ranking; otherwise it asks no registry, proposes nothing, leaves the filer's leads unexamined and lists the filer in the job's `filers_below_threshold` (CIK, filer, leads, best score, query, reasons). Universe filers are recorded as before.
  - **Counterparty filers.** Proposed once by the same rule (the Candidate is keyed by CIK); `Candidate.counterparty_company_id` (read-side, no schema change) names the counterparty a commit promotes.
  - **Prompts** `scout.v4` and `skeptic-plan.v4` (new files, same schemas; v3 kept): a specific phrase of two to four words in the query's own words, good and bad examples, the one-word rule, and a second quoted phrase beside a broad term.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/discovery/{edgar_fts,ranking,leads,service,handlers}.py`, `backend/atlas/candidates/{proposals,handlers,service}.py`, `backend/atlas/investigations/tasks.py` (`_take_leads` uses `discovery_sightings`; one artifact key; the docstring), `backend/atlas/roles/{scout,skeptic}.py`, `backend/atlas/roles/prompts/{scout.v4,skeptic-plan.v4}.md` (new), `backend/atlas/db/migrations/versions/0054_edgar_search_skipped.py` (new), `configs/discovery/lead-ranking.yaml` (version 3, `bare_term_factor`, `method_terms`), `frontend/lib/api/{openapi.json,schema.ts}` (regenerated: `EdgarSearch.status` `skipped` and `skip_reason`, `Candidate.counterparty_company_id`), `tests/fixtures/edgar-fts/{vcsel-lidar,silicon-photonics-lasers-nvidia}.json` (new, hand-written) and its manifest, `tests/fixtures/identity/{gleif/queries.json,openfigi/mapping.json,manifest.json}` (hand-written answers so NVIDIA resolves by CIK), `tests/fakes/edgar_fts.py` (docstring), `tests/unit/{test_edgar_fts,test_lead_ranking,test_bottleneck_prompts}.py`, `tests/integration/{test_candidates,test_edgar_discovery,test_counterparties,test_discovery,test_investigations,test_migrations}.py`, `AGENTS.md`, `docs/decisions.md`, `docs/runbooks.md`, the ticket.
- **Tests run** (WSL Ubuntu, the Compose Postgres and Silo, one module at a time; actual results): the unit tests of the phrase rule and of filing-lead scoring were written first and failed on import (`filing_phrase_skip_reason`, `matched`) before the code; the integration tests were written after the code they exercise, so no red run of them was seen. Final runs: `tests/unit/test_edgar_fts.py` + `test_lead_ranking.py` + `test_bottleneck_prompts.py` 74 passed; `tests/unit` 647 passed; `tests/integration/test_candidates.py` 10 passed (new: three queries in one `discover` job: `InP substrates` proposes Aeluma, the bare `VCSEL` is searched and its two hand-written lidar hits stay leads under the threshold with no registry request, `shortage` is recorded `skipped` and never sent); `test_edgar_discovery.py` 3 passed (the recorded fixture: all four leads kept at version 3, Aeluma's score 16.5 with its reasons, Aeluma still the one Candidate); `test_counterparties.py` 8 passed (new: NVIDIA, a counterparty, filing two hand-written hits, is one Candidate keyed by CIK with `counterparty_company_id`, and its commit promotes the row); `test_discovery.py` 15 passed; `test_investigations.py` 25 passed (run before the `Candidate` read change, which it does not touch); `test_theme_explorer.py` 13 passed; `test_migrations.py` 5 passed. `ruff format --check`, `ruff check` (backend, tests, scripts) and strict `pyright` (whole project) clean. Frontend: `typecheck`, `lint` and `test` (28) passed; the API client was regenerated with `scripts/gen_api_client.sh`.
- **Not run:** the other integration modules, the frontend build, the e2e and `scripts/ci.sh`: left to the runners. The live suites.
- **Fixture vs live:** fixture only; no EDGAR, SEC, GLEIF, OpenFIGI, SearXNG or LLM request was made. The two new EDGAR answers are hand-written (the lidar makers' names are the pilot's; their CIKs, accessions and dates are made up; NVIDIA's hits use its well-known CIK and made-up accessions). The pilot's own queries are not recorded in the repo, so the unit tests score the pilot's phrases against the re-run's recorded queries and hand-written ones, and the pairing of phrase and noise filer is by what the filer makes. Whether MiniMax writes specific phrases under `scout.v4`, and how many on-theme filers the threshold holds back, is unmeasured.
- **Deviations:**
  - The spec asks for a filing lead scored against its query and purpose. That alone does not put the pilot's two-word noise phrases under the threshold ("CW laser" scores 16.3 on a seven-term query), so ranking also demotes a hit of a bare vocabulary term. This is an addition to the spec's rule, chosen because it separates the pilot's useful phrases from its noise phrases exactly.
  - "A product or layer term of the lead-ranking config" is read as the config's `terms` without the bottleneck vocabulary, which moved to `method_terms`; the config's shape changed (ranking version 3).
  - A filer under the threshold is recorded in the job's artifacts, and its leads are left unexamined, rather than recorded as a mention with a new outcome (that would need a second schema change).
  - A counterparty filer gets no exemption from the threshold. The link to the counterparty is a read-side field, not a stored column.
  - `atlas.discovery.handlers` imports the `propose_candidates` kind inside `register_discovery_handlers`: `atlas.candidates` now imports `atlas.discovery` (ranking, sightings), and a module-level import would be circular.
  - The universe's names and website hosts given to ranking are computed in `atlas.candidates.handlers` as `atlas.investigations.tasks` computes them (two expressions repeated, to leave the Scout task's code alone for the tickets that change it).
  - `epiwafer`, one of the pilot's useful phrases, is no longer searched (one word, not in `terms`); the config's vocabulary was not extended.
- **Known limit:** the keep threshold is the web leads' and a filing lead shows only its phrase, so a specific phrase is kept only when the query has at most about eight topic terms and uses the phrase's words: `InP substrates` on "indium phosphide substrate export control China gallium germanium 2026" scores 13.0. The held-back filers are in `filers_below_threshold`.
- **Next:** the pilot re-run: compare each discovery's Candidates with its `filers_below_threshold`; if on-theme filers are held back, give filing leads their own threshold or score the phrase against the query's product terms only.
