## 2026-10-01: memory-directed reading ticket 08, a layer only when the quote supports one

- **Built** on ticket 02's branch (`docs/decisions.md`, "A layer only when the quote supports one; an edge is subject, predicate and object"; the ticket's "Resolution"):
  - **The layer rule** (`atlas.claims.layer_term`, `LAYER_TERMS`, `SHARED_LAYER_TERMS` in `atlas/claims/predicates.py`): each layer has its terms (the table is in the decision entry); a Claim's proposed layer is kept only when one of them occurs in the object text or the quote (for a product object, in the clauses naming the object); a word several layers share ("InP", "indium phosphide", "wafer", "chip") supports none; a term inside a longer term of another layer is not one ("laser drivers" is `dsp`). The Claim is accepted either way: `claim.layer` null, `claim.layer_term` (the supporting words) and `claim.layer_reason` `layer_unsupported`. A layer outside the taxonomy is still `unknown_layer`. The Investigator's response schema lets `layer` be null.
  - **The same rule at review** for any Assertion (`review_relationships`): an Assertion with a layer its quote doesn't name, or with none, proposes a layerless edge (it was `not_eligible` `unknown_layer` without one).
  - **Edge identity (subject, predicate, object).** Evidence bringing another layer than its edge has joins it, is recorded `needs_human_review` with `layer_conflict`, and sends a `machine_reviewed` edge to the exceptions queue (`relationship.layer_conflict`); no second edge. An edge with no layer takes the first layer later Evidence brings (`relationship.layer_set`). A conflicted edge is not lifted by a later passing witness.
  - **Reviewer `reviewer.v4`** (v3 kept): `direction`, `hedge`, `layer` (`not_proposed` for an item with none), no overall `verdict`. Reasons: `direction_not_confirmed`, `reviewer_hedged`, and `layer_not_confirmed` only for an Assertion with a layer. Direction confirmed, no hedge and no layer is `machine_reviewed` with a null layer.
  - **Investigator `investigator.v7`** (v6 kept): the layer may be null, and when. Both roles are sent each layer's `terms` (`LayerOption.terms`).
  - **Migration `0055`** (`down_revision` `0052`; the lead re-chains): `claim.layer` nullable, `claim.layer_term`, `claim.layer_reason`; `relationship.layer` nullable, the unique constraint with the layer replaced by the unique index `uq_relationship_identity` on (subject, predicate, object key) over the rows that are not `legacy_layer_duplicate` (new column: the migration marks all but the oldest of each group of existing edges that differ only by layer; no layer is rewritten); the identity trigger lets the layer change only from null, once; `relationship_review.reviewer_hedge`, `supported_layer`, `reviewer_layer` gains `not_proposed`, and "answered" is keyed on the direction (new reviews have no `reviewer_verdict`). Its downgrade refuses while a layerless Claim or Relationship, or a per-check review, exists.
  - **Read side:** `Relationship.layer` nullable; `GET /api/v1/relationships?layer=none`; sorted by layer, layerless edges first; **the owner's report** `GET /api/v1/relationships/layer-duplicates` (an endpoint, read-only: the groups of edges that differ only by layer, oldest first); the theme map's `relationships` hold layerless edges first. Frontend: the edge table's layer filter gains "No layer"; `LayerName` shows "none"; the Theme explorer lists layerless edges under each theme company they name (`lib/themes.ts`) and only layered ones in its by-layer table; an edge's Evidence shows the Reviewer's three answers and the layer its quote supports; the investigation page shows "no layer".

The gold cases are pinned and untouched; the evaluation's script stub turns their `verdict` into the per-check answer.
  - Beyond the ticket's rule: for a product object the quote is read only in the object's clause; a term inside a longer term of another layer doesn't count; and the rule is applied again at review, so Assertions recorded before it (and researchers') form layerless edges too.
  - Decided here: an Assertion brings its layer to the edge only if the Reviewer, when it answered, confirmed its direction and its layer; an edge with no layer takes a layer once (the ticket only says an edge may have none); a `layer_conflict` edge is lifted only by the owner, and an owner-decided edge keeps its state when a conflict arrives (the reason shows on it); layerless edges sort before the layers (so the unrun e2e's sort expectation holds).

  - The owner's report is an API endpoint and a runbook section ("Edges that differ only by layer"); there is no page for it.

## 2026-10-01: memory-directed reading ticket 05, passage selection

- **Built** (`docs/decisions.md`, "Passage selection: pointers and search, alternately", first titled "…, best first"; it replaces "The passage budget is spread over the documents (pilot fix 10)"). What follows is the first build (commit `21c0d42`); the **Amendment** paragraph at the end of this entry changes the order and the floor:
  - **`atlas.claims.selection`** (new, pure: no database, no model, no Memory call). A document's windows (the sectioner's sections cut at 3,000 characters, as before) become candidates through four selections: `pointer:<query_index>` (for each reading pointer into the document, the window of the pointed section sharing most terms with the pointer's Memory text; the section found by its anchor in the parse read), `search` (a window containing a term of the question or of the Scout's queries, scored by BM25 over all the extraction's windows), `entity:<company_id>` (as before; ranked by its search score) and `lead` (only for a document with none of those). A window several selections choose is one passage listing them all. `deal` takes them best first (pointer windows by rank, then search and entity windows by score, then lead windows) with a floor of one passage for each periodic report's primary document and each results release (an 8-K with Item 2.02, its EX-99.1 and EX-99.2) that has a candidate, a ceiling per document, the ceiling yielding before the budget is stranded, and lead windows after everything else. The section-wide `recall` selection is gone.
  - **`atlas.research.search`** (new): the tokenizer, the passage splitter and BM25 moved from `atlas.evaluation.baseline`, which now wraps them and keeps its names; plus `overlap`, how a pointer's Memory text finds its window.
  - **Setting** `ATLAS_INVESTIGATOR_MAX_PASSAGE_SHARE_PER_DOCUMENT` (`investigator_max_passage_share_per_document`, default 1/3, 0 < share ≤ 1): the ceiling as a share of the extraction's passage budget, rounded down, at least 1; for Investigator tasks and the standalone job alike.
  - **The Investigator's own recall is removed** (`ClaimExtractor` takes no `recall`; `claim_extractor(settings, engine, caller, runs, max_passages=)` no longer takes the gateway). An Investigator task reads the round's pointers into its documents and the round's Scout queries (`atlas.investigations.pointers.round_reading`) and passes them to `ClaimExtractor.extract(job, payload, reading)`; the standalone `extract_claims` job passes none and selects by search, entity and lead. No Memory text reaches a role; no prompt changed.
  - **Read side.** `GET /api/v1/claims[/{id}]`: `passage_selected_by` (the Claim's passage's `selected_by`, computed from its extraction; `[]` when it names no passage sent). The investigation's `evidence` entries: the same. The card's `read` documents: `selections` (passages per kind). Extraction and Investigator-task artifacts: `passages_by_selection`. Research Snapshot: `memory.pointer_selections` (the passages a pointer chose; `recall_selections` stays for extractions made before this). The investigation page shows a document's selections in "What was read" and each Evidence item's ("passage selected by pointer, search").
  - **No migration**: `claim_extraction.passages` is JSON; only its `selected_by` values changed.
  - **Hindsight fake:** `script_fact_text(document_id, text)`: a derived fact in the test's own words (a paraphrase), listed with the fake's derivations.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/claims/{selection.py (new),extraction.py,handlers.py,reads.py,__init__.py}`, `backend/atlas/research/search.py` (new), `backend/atlas/evaluation/baseline.py`, `backend/atlas/investigations/{pointers.py,tasks.py,coverage.py,model.py}`, `backend/atlas/snapshots.py`, `backend/atlas/settings.py`, `.env.example`, `frontend/app/investigation/page.tsx`, `frontend/lib/workbench.ts`, `frontend/unit/workbench.test.ts`, `frontend/lib/api/{openapi.json,schema.ts}` (regenerated), `scripts/{investigator_replay.py,e2e_hypothesis.py}`, `tests/fakes/hindsight.py`, `tests/unit/{test_passage_selection.py (new),test_hindsight_fake_derivations.py}`, `tests/integration/{test_claims,test_investigations,test_hypotheses,test_scenarios}.py`, `tests/live/{test_extraction_smoke_live.py,extraction_smoke.py}`, `AGENTS.md`, `docs/{decisions,data-model,runbooks,implementation-log}.md`, the ticket.
- **Tests run** (WSL, the Compose Postgres and Silo, one module at a time; actual results):
  - `tests/unit/test_passage_selection.py` 36 passed (new: where a pointer leads, also with no shared term, a missing anchor and another document's pointer; search over the question's and the queries' terms; entity tags; a window chosen by four selections is one candidate and one passage; lead windows only for a document with nothing else, an 8-K's results Item first; the order across kinds; ties; the ceiling; the unused share passing on; the floor, also for a lead-only release and with a budget smaller than the floors; a lead-only document with no floor waiting for every other candidate; lead-only documents taking turns; `dropped`; which documents have the floor, 14 cases; the ceiling's rounding; `overlap` and `bm25`). `tests/unit/test_archive_baseline.py` 7 passed, **unchanged**. `tests/unit/test_hindsight_fake_derivations.py` 16 passed (one new: `script_fact_text` changes only the fact's text). `tests/unit` 730 passed.
  - `tests/integration/test_claims.py` 21 passed. New or rewritten: the standalone job makes no recall and its first passage is the window holding the 10-K's peer-group sentence for a question made of that sentence's rare words (`search` and `entity:<Lumentum>` on one passage; entity-only windows after every search window; 24 passages, the rest counted as dropped); six passages over Coherent's 10-K and 10-Q and Lumentum's 10-K and EX-99.1 give each 10-K two (the ceiling) and the 10-Q and the release one each (the floor; the 10-Q's is a lead window); a Claim's `passage_selected_by` equals its passage's `selected_by`, and is `[]` for an unknown passage.

New (replacing fix 10's spread test), on the recorded Lumentum filings with one fact in Memory that paraphrases the allocation statement and a hand-shaped administrative 8-K: the first passage is the window of the 10-K's Item 1 holding "This demand is outpacing our current supply", selected by `pointer:0`–`pointer:3` (not Item 1's opening window), and the only pointer passage; the Memory text is in no role request; all 30 passages of the budget are spent, the other 29 on search windows; the 8-K with Item 2.02, the EX-99.1 and the 10-Q each have at least one; the 10-K has more than the ceiling of 10 (the unused share passed on) and the EX-99.1 at most 10; the administrative 8-K has none while candidates were dropped; the accepted `capacity_constrained` Claim and its Evidence-tray entry carry the passage's selections; the card's `read` shows `selections` per document. Changed: the two tests that counted recalls now expect only the Scout's theme recalls (three and four); the answered-card test also checks the Evidence entry's selections; the Evidence Family test gives the Investigator 60 passages and quotes the 10-K by version (see deviations).
  - `tests/integration/test_hypotheses.py` 12 passed (its fixture gives the Investigator 48 passages; the snapshot test now expects `pointer_selections` and an empty `recall_selections`), `test_scenarios.py` 6 passed (the Lumentum fixture raises `investigation_max_passages` too). Unchanged and passing: `test_proposed_updates.py` 3, `test_investigation_follow_up.py` 6, `test_counterparties.py` 8, `test_evaluations.py` 4, `test_relationships.py` 13, `test_reparse.py` 5, `test_retention_triage.py` 14, `test_edgar_discovery.py` 3, `test_queue_pause.py` 11, `test_quota_pacing.py` 6, `test_theme_explorer.py` 13, `test_parser_versions.py` 2, `test_pdf_sources.py` 7, `test_migrations.py` 6.
  - **Red runs.** The three extraction-seam tests were run before the extraction changed and failed as intended (a recall was made; the equal deal gave the two 10-Ks 2 and 1 of the 6; no `passage_selected_by`). The frontend test failed first (`selectionSummary is not a function`). The selection's unit tests were written with the module, so no red run of them was seen. The investigation acceptance test was written before the wiring but first run after it; a mutation was run instead: with the pointers not passed to the extraction, it fails on the first passage (the search's best window, not the statement's); restored.
  - First full runs after the change, before the test fixes: `test_investigations.py` 31 passed, 1 failed (the Evidence Family test: with a byte copy of the 10-K among four documents, 24 passages were all pointer windows and the supply-agreement window wasn't sent); `test_hypotheses.py` 2 failed (the second quoted window, "NVIDIA made a $2 billion investment", not sent within 24; the snapshot's `recall_selections`); `test_scenarios.py` 1 failed (Lumentum's allocation statement not sent). All three causes are the same: the fake recalls a fact for every retained section for every query, so each section's opening window is a pointer window and fills a document's ceiling before search windows.
  - `ruff format --check`, `ruff check` (backend, tests, scripts) and strict `pyright` (whole project): clean. Frontend: `lint` and `typecheck` clean, `test` 32 passed. The API client was regenerated with the script's two commands (the CRLF checkout stops WSL running `scripts/gen_api_client.sh`).
  - Rehearsals of the two opt-in tools that used to stub the recall (scripted fake, localhost only): `scripts/investigator_replay.py --rehearse --variant v6` ran: 24 passages (Item 1: 9, Item 1A: 10, Item 7: 5), 4 calls, 19 proposed, 15 accepted. `tests/live/test_extraction_smoke_live.py` with `ATLAS_LIVE_TESTS=rehearse`: the three extractions run and choose passages (3 calls), then **its rehearsal check fails** on `("supplies", "rejected", "exactly_once", "accepted")`: the scripted quote one character off is located and accepted, which is the rule since "Claim quotes are located, not trusted" (2026-09-29). That expectation was stale before this ticket and is not touched here.
- **Not run:** the full integration suite, the e2e (`scripts/e2e_hypothesis.py` edited by hand: the Investigator's budget raised to 48 so its one call reaches both quoted windows; `scripts/e2e.py` unchanged, its supply-agreement window is within 24 by a simulation of the selection, not by a run), the frontend build, `scripts/ci.sh`, the live suites, the live verification's rehearsal.
- **Fixture-only vs live:** nothing live; no LLM, Hindsight, SEC or SearXNG call. The Memory text of the acceptance test is written by the test (`script_fact_text`), and its administrative 8-K is a hand-shaped manual import, labelled so (the recorded Lumentum fixtures hold only the results 8-K); the filings are the recorded ones. Whether real recalls rank the right facts first, how many pointer windows a real round gives a document, and whether term overlap finds the right window for Hindsight's real wording are unmeasured: the pilot's re-run of investigation 1 measures them (`passages_by_selection`, `passage_selected_by`).
- **Deviations and readings of the ticket:**
  - **The ceiling yields before the budget is stranded** (not in the ticket): when every document is at its ceiling or out of candidates, the candidates passed over are taken best first. Without it a lone 10-K would be read with a third of the budget, and leftover budget would go unread.
  - **Lead windows of a document with no floor come after every other candidate**, the over-the-ceiling ones included, so the administrative 8-K gets none while any pointer, search or entity candidate is unread (the acceptance box read literally).
  - **Entity windows rank by their search score** (0 with no match): "search and entity windows by score" with one score. Before, entity-tagged windows came first; now a window naming NVIDIA competes on the question's terms. In the tests this is why two modules needed a larger Investigator budget.
  - **Every window with a matching term is a `search` candidate**; there is no relevance bar and no top-N. So `search` on a pointer passage says the window also matches a term, not that search alone would have read it.
  - The floor is for primary documents of the periodic forms (and 8-K results releases with their EX-99.1/EX-99.2), read from `source_version.metadata.items`; a 6-K's exhibits and documents without a form have none.
  - The Research Snapshot key `memory.pointer_selections` and the page's display are additions the ticket doesn't list; the snapshot's `recall_selections` would otherwise have become silently empty.
  - `scripts/investigator_replay.py` and the live smoke test no longer stub a recall: their passages are the search selection's, so a replay is not comparable with the passages `docs/research/investigator-yield.md` was measured on. This is the documented behaviour the removal of the recall changes; it was replaced, not kept.
  - The ceiling forgives a hundredth of a passage when rounding down, so `0.3333` in the environment is a third.
  - Existing tests in `test_hypotheses.py`, `test_scenarios.py` and one in `test_investigations.py` were given a larger `investigation_max_passages` rather than a scripted Memory, to keep their subjects (drafting, scenarios, Evidence Families) apart from selection.
- **Known limits** (decision entry): pointers are trusted without a rank cut-off and can fill the budget; no check that the chosen window holds the fact; repeated text across documents is not deduplicated; the search has no relevance bar.
- **Next:** ticket 06 (Investigators for the companies the pointers name; document choice by pointer) calls the same `round_reading` and `extract(job, payload, reading)`; ticket 07 (the Skeptic) can call `atlas.claims.selection.select` with its own documents and pointers, and should give its pointers' `query_index` a meaning of its own before they reach `selected_by`. The stale rehearsal check of the extraction smoke test wants a ticket. The pilot re-run: count accepted Claims by `passage_selected_by`.
- **Amendment (2026-10-01, from the lead, a second commit on `21c0d42`): pointer windows no longer crowd out search windows; call transcripts have the floor.**
  - **Why.** The first build took every pointer window before any search window, and its deviation said so: with production's 72 passages and plentiful pointers the Investigator would read mostly pointer passages. Since then production gained 43 earnings-call and conference transcripts (provider `tradingview`, parser `tradingview-transcript-v1`, Tier B, document types "Call transcript" and "Event transcript"). They are archived but not yet retained into Hindsight (the Codex budget is held), so they have no pointers and only the search reaches them; they hold the pilot's best statements ("our EML capacity, which is completely sold out", "the industry-wide constraint in indium phosphide"), and a re-run of investigation 1 took 23 of its 32 accepted Claims from transcripts. Recall and the term search are two independent channels, and both must get read.
  - **What changed** (`atlas.claims.selection.deal`). After the floor, one pointer candidate (by rank), then one search or entity candidate (by score), and so on, under the per-document ceiling; when one list runs out the other goes on; a window in both lists is one passage and is taken once. The over-the-ceiling step alternates the same way; lead windows stay last. The passages are stored in that reading order (pointer and other windows alternately, then leads). The floor also covers a results-call transcript: `source_document.provider` `tradingview` with `document_type` "Call transcript" (how `atlas.tradingview.service` records the catalog's category title; compared without case); "Event transcript" has none. `selection.Document` and the extraction's version read gained `provider`.
  - **Files:** `backend/atlas/claims/{selection,extraction}.py`, `backend/atlas/investigations/tasks.py` and `backend/atlas/settings.py` (comments), `tests/unit/test_passage_selection.py`, `AGENTS.md`, `docs/{decisions,data-model,implementation-log}.md`, the ticket. No API, schema or frontend change.
  - **Tests** (WSL, one module at a time; actual results). `tests/unit/test_passage_selection.py` 47 passed: 11 new or rewritten cases were red first (13 failures before the change, the six parametrized transcript cases among them): the alternation with all kinds present; ties; more pointer windows than the budget beside a document with no pointer (8 passages: 4 and 4, in turn); one list running out, either way; a window both channels chose taken once; the ceiling counting a document's pointer and search passages together, then yielding in turn; which transcripts have the floor; a call transcript with one weak search window read beside a better-matching filing while a conference transcript is not. `tests/unit` 741 passed. `test_claims.py` 21 passed, `test_investigations.py` 32 passed, `test_hypotheses.py` 12 passed, `test_scenarios.py` 6 passed. `ruff format --check`, `ruff check` and strict `pyright`: clean.
  - **Expectations changed:** only in `tests/unit/test_passage_selection.py` (the order test and the ties test now expect the two channels in turn). No integration expectation changed: the acceptance test has one pointer window, which is still the first passage, and the tests under the fake's recalls had budgets that reach their quoted windows either way.
  - **Measured on the recorded fixtures** (the fake's recalls, 24 passages): Lumentum's four filings 13 pointer and 11 search windows, the 10-K's eight now 5 pointer and 3 search windows (first build: 8 pointer windows). `scripts/e2e_hypothesis.py` still needs its 48 passages (simulated, not run: at 24 the second quoted window is not reached).
  - **Not tested at a seam:** the transcript floor is unit-tested (the rule, and `select` on hand-built documents). No integration test puts a `tradingview` transcript through an extraction: the synthetic TradingView fixtures call their category "Earnings call", not "Call transcript", so a fixture transcript has no floor. Whether production's category title is exactly "Call transcript" is the lead's statement, not checked here.
  - **Not run:** the other integration modules (they passed on the first build; the alternation changes which passages their Investigators read), the e2e, the frontend gates (no frontend change).

## 2026-10-01: Hindsight's metadata routing prototyped (LIVE on a local Hindsight); home-ops PR #7180

- **Asked by the owner:** whether Hindsight could alternate between ChatGPT and MiniMax, to lift the intake limit of the shared ChatGPT quota. The owner agreed to the lead's recommendation: Hindsight's `metadata` strategy, which routes only the retained items that ask for it.
- **Prototype** (pilot-review ticket 18; its comment has the details): a throwaway Compose Hindsight **0.10.2** on this machine, LLM calls through the owner's LiteLLM to MiniMax (six small retain extractions; the containers and banks were removed afterwards). Routing by the item's `extractor` metadata works, also inside a mixed batch; a routed item whose member fails makes its operation fail and does not fall back. Not verified: a chain whose primary is `openai-codex`.
- **Home-ops PR #7180** (draft, for the k8s team): a second chain member (MiniMax-M3 through LiteLLM, thinking off) and the strategy on the shared `llm/hindsight`, reusing its LiteLLM key. Inert until a client sends the key.
- **Atlas side:** ticket 11 of the memory-directed reading spec (`ATLAS_RETAIN_EXTRACTOR`, the routed retains' own budget, the extractor recorded per section). Not built yet.
- **Production facts read today:** 473 sections retained, 1,907 retained-section outcomes recorded as failed (not yet diagnosed), 44 retains held by the Codex cap; 43 TradingView transcripts in the archive (Lumentum 20, Coherent 13, AXT 10), none retained yet. A re-run of investigation 1 on 0.2.5 after the budget raise and the transcripts (`daf5720c-838b-4cd7-a850-898687b89140`, unreviewed): 32 Claims accepted of 130 proposed (10 of 25 before), 23 of the 32 from transcripts, 499k tokens, 9.6 minutes.
## 2026-10-01: memory-directed reading ticket 10, the baseline drops near-duplicates

- **Built** (the reasoning is in the docstring of `atlas.evaluation.baseline`; the ticket's "Resolution"):
  - **`search` drops near-duplicates.** Two passages say nearly the same when the Jaccard overlap of their sets of stemmed terms (stopwords included, the ranking's own `tokens`) is at least `NEAR_DUPLICATE` = 0.75 and their documents were available at different times. Passages are still taken best first. The first wording of a statement takes with it every passage that says nearly the same; a passage met later that says nearly the same as a hit shown is dropped for it. The hit shows the wording of the newest of those documents that hasn't shown its `per_document` hits yet; a dropped wording uses none of its document's share. The hits are returned in the order of their own scores. The BM25 corpus is unchanged (near-duplicates still count in its statistics), so every score is what it was.
  - **`Hit.also_in`**: the source version ids of the other documents whose passage was dropped for the hit, newest first; the documents of word-for-word copies (dropped as before) are listed too.
  - **`scripts/pilot_baseline.py`**: each row of `results.json` has `also_in` (`source_version_id`, `company`, `title`, `available_at` of each other document); `summary.md` shows "Also in N other documents: <titles>" above the passage and a "Repeats" line in its head. No argument changed.
  - **Cost**: a hit is compared once with every passage that has a term of the question, and each passage walked with the hits so far; no passage-with-passage comparison.
- **How the threshold was chosen** (offline, from files in the lead's checkout that are not in git: the four saved baselines of `.scratch/live-runs/breadth-0.2.5/` and the 302 cached parsed texts of `.scratch/live-runs/baseline-cache/`; no request was made). Among the 80 saved hits: the export-permit paragraph in four AXT filings overlaps 0.86 to 1.00; the lowest pairs that are still one paragraph repeated are 0.79 (the same paragraph with the short one before it joined to its passage) and 0.80 (the next filing's version with two sentences added); the highest pairs that say different things are 0.67 and 0.68 (the paragraph before the first permits were granted, and a later version); no pair lies between 0.68 and 0.79; every other pair is at 0.64 or below. The fifth of investigation 2's "five" hits is the oldest filing's earlier version (0.57 to 0.59 from the other four) and stays its own hit. Without stopwords the gap is 0.65 to 0.72; with three-word shingles 0.56 to 0.67. There is no clean valley in the wider archive: among the 300 best passages for each question over all 302 cached documents, 13 to 21 pairs per question lie between 0.70 and 0.75.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/evaluation/baseline.py`, `scripts/pilot_baseline.py`, `tests/unit/test_archive_baseline.py`, `AGENTS.md`, `.claude/skills/pilot-review/SKILL.md` (one sentence in step 3), `docs/implementation-log.md`, the ticket. No migration, no API or prompt change; `atlas.research.search` and `atlas.claims.selection` are untouched.
- **Tests run** (WSL; actual results):
  - **Red first:** the seven new cases failed before `search` changed (three hits for the reworded paragraph, no `also_in`), the seven existing ones and `test_passage_selection.py` passed: 7 failed, 54 passed.
  - `tests/unit/test_archive_baseline.py` 14 passed: the 7 existing tests **unchanged**, and 7 new cases: the paragraph reworded in three filings (two dates, a figure, one added sentence) is one hit from the newest filing naming the other two, whether the oldest or the newest wording scores best (two cases); an earlier version of the paragraph (overlap 0.63 to 0.68 with its wordings) and another paragraph on the subject (0.18 to 0.19) stay hits of their own beside it; word-for-word copies' documents are named, a dropped wording's copy too; a dropped wording doesn't use its document's share; when the newest filing has shown its share the paragraph is shown from the filing before it, which names both others; passages of one filing and of its exhibit are not compared. The paragraphs are hand-shaped on the AXT case, not the filings' text.
  - `tests/unit/test_passage_selection.py` 47 passed (unchanged). `tests/unit` 798 passed. `ruff format --check`, `ruff check` (backend, tests, scripts) and strict `pyright` (whole project): clean.
  - **Mutations** (scratch scripts, not committed): threshold 0.65, the different-paragraphs test fails; threshold 0.9, four of the new tests fail; the rule without "available at different times" (any two passages compared, or any two documents), the existing `test_one_document_gives_at_most_its_share_of_the_hits` fails.
  - **Offline rehearsal** (scratch scripts over the cached parses; not a baseline run): `search` over the documents investigation 2's saved hits came from (10 documents, so the scores are not the saved run's) gives the export-permit paragraph once, from the 10-Q of 2026-08-13, naming four other filings; 12 of the 20 hits name other documents. The script's row and summary code was run on that result. Over all 302 cached documents (19,037 passages, 3,068 to 4,041 with a term of the question) one search takes about 2.5 s with or without the dropping, which makes 61,000 to 81,000 comparisons.
- **Fixture-only vs live:** nothing live. `scripts/pilot_baseline.py` was **not run** against production or any API; its `main` is untested here. The threshold's evidence is the saved output of the unreviewed breadth runs, read from disk.
- **Deviations and readings of the ticket:**
  - **One filing repeating itself, and a filing and its exhibit, are not compared.** The existing test `test_one_document_gives_at_most_its_share_of_the_hits` had to pass unchanged, and its passages are near-duplicates by any measure: six of one 10-K and one of an 8-K of the same time, two words apart (overlap 0.90 to 0.95), expected as 3 + 1 hits. So only documents available at different times are compared, which is the pilot's case (a filing restating an earlier one). What it leaves: in the rehearsal, investigation 3's list still has two passages of one EX-99.2 at 0.97 and investigation 5's two of one 10-K at 0.97. Dropping those too is one condition in `_near_duplicates`, and that test's passages would have to become different statements. The lead's call.
  - **Dropped at the hit stage, not while the documents are read.** The lead's note pictured the exact-duplicate rule extended (newest first, so the newest is kept). That would compare every passage with every earlier one, and with `per_document` it would hide every repeated paragraph beyond the newest filing's three. Here the passages are ranked as before and the newest wording is chosen when the hit is made.
  - **"The newest document's passage is kept" has one exception:** a document that has shown its `per_document` hits shows no more, so the statement is shown from the newest document with a hit to spare and names the newer one in `also_in` (in the rehearsal: three of investigation 2's hits are shown from the 10-Q of May 2026 and name the August one).
  - `also_in` lists word-for-word copies' documents too (the ticket says "dropped duplicates"); in `results.json` its entries are objects, not bare ids; the summary's "Repeats" line is an addition.
- **Known limits** (docstring): "nearly the same" is not transitive, so of three versions drifting apart the third can be its own hit; pairs just under the line stay (the rehearsal has two, at 0.73 and 0.74); a word-for-word copy's statement is still lost when the newest document has shown its share (as before this ticket: copies are dropped while the documents are read).
- **Next:** the reviewed runs on the memory-directed reading release use this baseline; the reviewer counts a hit's `also_in` documents as the same hit. If one filing's own repeats should go too, reshape the cap test and remove the time condition.

## 2026-10-01: home-ops PR #7180 merged; the route checked on the shared Hindsight (LIVE)

The owner merged PR #7180. The shared `llm/hindsight` (0.10.2) rolled out with the second chain member and the `metadata` strategy and started cleanly (log: "Connection verified: openai/MiniMax-M3"). Checked through its API with the development key, in a scratch bank deleted afterwards: one small item with `extractor: minimax` was extracted by `MiniMax-M3` (provider `openai`), one without the key by `gpt-5.6-luna` (provider `openai-codex`), and consolidation ran on `gpt-5.6-luna`. Two retain extractions and one consolidation call in all. Atlas does not send the key yet (ticket 11 of the memory-directed reading spec is being built), so nothing in the research bank is routed.

## 2026-10-01: memory-directed reading ticket 09, a company-level constraint needs no named product

- **Built** on `integrate/mdr` (tickets 01-04 and 08) (`docs/decisions.md`, "A company-level constraint needs no named product"; the ticket's "Resolution"):
  - **The rule** (`atlas.claims.company_level`, `COMPANY_LEVEL_PREDICATES` in `atlas/claims/predicates.py`): a `capacity_constrained` Claim proposed with no object text, or with one that names no product (generic words and the words of the constraint itself), is company-level: no object, no layer. At extraction it is accepted when the subject is the document's filer (`company_level_not_filer`), named, in the first person or as the unnamed party of its own impersonal sentence (`party_basis` `filer`), the quote names no other company (`other_company_in_quote`; `party_not_in_quote` when the filer is unnamed) and it carries a constraint cue. Lumentum's allocation sentence proposed with "our products" (rejected `object_not_in_quote` in breadth investigation 5) is now an accepted Claim. A named, specific object and the other three bottleneck predicates are as before.
  - **Recorded:** `claim.company_level` (true only on an accepted company-level Claim; `object_text`, `layer` and `layer_reason` null, the proposal in `claim.proposed`); the Assertion's `value_json.company_level`.
  - **The edge:** `relationship` with neither object company nor object text and no layer; `object_key` the reserved empty string, so `uq_relationship_identity` holds one company-level edge per company and every further such Assertion joins it. The same rule at review for any Assertion (`not_eligible` `company_level_not_filer` when the subject is not the filer).
  - **The hedge check** is unchanged: the risk factor's "could" is `needs_human_review` `hedged_language` without a Reviewer call; the allocation sentence, confirmed by the Reviewer, is `machine_reviewed`.
  - **Prompts:** `investigator.v8` and `reviewer.v5` (v7 and v4 kept); the Reviewer's item gains `company_level`; the `reads` of `capacity_constrained` says "with no object ...".
  - **Migration `0057`** (`down_revision` `0055`; the lead re-chains): `claim.company_level` with its check; on `relationship`, 0021's two unnamed checks replaced by `relationship_object_check`, `relationship_object_key_check` and `relationship_company_level_check`. No existing row changes. Its downgrade refuses while a company-level Claim or Relationship exists.
  - **Read side:** `Relationship.company_level` and `Claim.company_level` on the API; sorted by object a company-level edge comes first; a proposed update about one names it by subject and predicate. Frontend: `objectLabel` and `edgeLabel` in `lib/relationships.ts` ("(company-level)" for no object), `EdgeObject` shows "company-level" (edge table, exceptions, edge page, dossier, Theme explorer), the Evidence tray labels a Claim with no object the same way.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/claims/{predicates,extraction,reads,__init__}.py`, `backend/atlas/relationships/{review,service,reads}.py`, `backend/atlas/roles/{investigator,reviewer}.py`, `backend/atlas/roles/prompts/{investigator.v8,reviewer.v5}.md` (new), `backend/atlas/db/migrations/versions/0057_company_level_constraint.py` (new), `backend/atlas/{themes,dossier}.py` (docstrings and a field description), `backend/atlas/proposed_updates/detection.py` (one line), `frontend/lib/relationships.ts`, `frontend/components/relationships.tsx`, `frontend/app/{investigation,theme}/page.tsx`, `frontend/unit/{relationships,themes}.test.ts`, `frontend/lib/api/{openapi.json,schema.ts}` (regenerated), `tests/unit/{test_claim_predicates,test_relationship_checks,test_bottleneck_prompts}.py`, `tests/integration/{test_claims,test_relationships,test_theme_explorer,test_migrations}.py`, `tests/integration/test_counterparties.py` (the prompt version), `docs/{decisions,data-model,implementation-log}.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `CONTEXT.md`, the ticket.
- **Tests (actual results, under WSL, one module at a time):**
  - `tests/unit` 769 passed (the whole directory in one run). In it: `test_claim_predicates.py` 154 (25 new cases: only `capacity_constrained` may be company-level; no object, "our products", "manufacturing capacity", "our current supply", "supply allocation" and seven more name no product; five named products do; the other predicates never are; the allocation sentence's cue); `test_relationship_checks.py` 38 (1 new: the allocation sentence is explicit, "we could experience supply constraints" and the 10-K's risk factor are hedged by "could", the expansion sentence has no cue; the Reviewer at v5 with the new phrases); `test_bottleneck_prompts.py` 8 (the Investigator at v8 with the new phrases).
  - `tests/integration/test_claims.py` 24 passed (2 new, 1 changed, at the extraction seam through the CLI, a worker pass and the API). On the recorded Lumentum FY2026 10-K: the allocation sentence proposed with "our products" and layer `module`, and with no object, is accepted with `company_level` true, no object, no layer, cue "demand is outpacing"; proposed for Coherent it is `company_level_not_filer`; the risk factor's conditional with "manufacturing capacity" is accepted company-level; "EML laser chips" is still `object_not_in_quote`; `sole_sources` with no object is still `missing_object`; the request's `reads` says "with no object" for `capacity_constrained` only. On a hand-written Lumentum document: a slide bullet naming nobody is accepted (`party_basis` `filer`), "Our contract manufacturer Fabrinet is capacity constrained ..." is `other_company_in_quote`, "Fabrinet remains supply constrained ..." is `party_not_in_quote`. Changed: "We are experiencing supply constraints due to constrained manufacturing capacity" with object "manufacturing capacity" was `generic_object` and is now an accepted company-level Claim; the expansion sentence is still `no_directional_language`.
  - `tests/integration/test_relationships.py` 19 passed (2 new, at the review seam). On the Lumentum 10-K: the risk factor's Claim forms the company-level edge in the exceptions queue (`hedged_language`, no Reviewer call); the allocation sentence, confirmed by the Reviewer (its item has `company_level` true and no object or layer), joins the same edge and makes it `machine_reviewed`; the statement in another filing (a hand-shaped document) adds Evidence to the same edge (3 Evidence, 2 families, one edge); the edge is listed by company, by `layer=none` and by predicate, on the dossier's `relationships_out` and on the theme map; `atlas audit verify` passes. A researcher's Assertions on Coherent's 10-K: no object and "manufacturing capacity" form one company-level edge, "indium phosphide capacity" its own product edge, NVIDIA as subject is `not_eligible` `company_level_not_filer`, `sole_sources` with no object `missing_object`.
  - `tests/integration/test_theme_explorer.py` 14 passed (1 new: the map and the dossier hold a company-level edge with no object, before the layered edge, and it is no other company's `relationships_in`). `tests/integration/test_migrations.py` 8 passed (1 new: after `0057` one edge with no object can be inserted per company, and a second, one with a layer, one of another predicate, a text with the empty key and no text with a key are each refused by the constraint named; the edge recorded before is unchanged; `claim.company_level` defaults to false).
  - Modules whose scripted Claims and reviews pass through the changed code, unchanged apart from the prompt version: `test_counterparties.py` 8 passed, `test_scenarios.py` 6, `test_evaluations.py` 4 (the gold cases in fake mode), `test_hypotheses.py` 12, `test_proposed_updates.py` 3, `test_edgar_discovery.py` 3, `test_investigation_follow_up.py` 6, `test_investigations.py` 32, `test_parser_versions.py` 2, `test_reparse.py` 5, `test_pdf_sources.py` 7, `test_queue_pause.py` 11, `test_quota_pacing.py` 6.
  - **Red runs:** the three extraction-seam tests were run before the extraction change and failed (the allocation sentence `rejected` `object_not_in_quote`, the pilot's own rejection; `KeyError: 'company_level'` in the other two); the two frontend label tests were red before `lib/relationships.ts` changed (2 failed, 37 passed). The unit tests were written before the code but first run after it, and the review-seam tests were first run after the review change; two mutations instead, each restored: company-level eligibility disabled at review (both new review tests fail: no edge for the risk factor, 2 Reviewer items instead of 3), and a non-reserved object key for the company-level edge (the database refuses it: `relationship_object_key_check`).
  - `ruff format --check`, `ruff check` and strict `pyright` clean for the whole project; on the regenerated client `npm --prefix frontend run lint` and `typecheck` clean and `test` 39 passed (3 new).
- **Not run:** the full suite, the e2e and `scripts/e2e.py` (not edited: they seed no company-level edge, and every other label is unchanged), the frontend build, `scripts/gen_api_client.sh --check` (the client was regenerated with the script's two commands), the live suites and their rehearsals, `scripts/investigator_replay.py`, the migration's downgrade.
- **Fixture-only vs live:** nothing live; no LLM, Hindsight or SEC call. The allocation sentence and the risk factor are in the recorded Lumentum FY2026 10-K; the sentence is read there as a recall hit of a question, with the passage budget raised so the whole filing is sent in one call (passage selection is ticket 05's and untouched). "Another filing" is a hand-shaped Lumentum document imported by hand (the recorded fixtures hold no second Lumentum filing with the statement), and the slide bullet and the two Fabrinet sentences are written for the test, all labelled so. Whether MiniMax follows `investigator.v8` and `reviewer.v5` is unmeasured.
- **Deviations:**
  - "Names no other company as the constrained party" is implemented as "names no other company": who the constrained party is can't be told deterministically. A sentence stating the filer's own constraint and naming a customer is therefore refused.
  - Beyond the ticket's generic objects, an object made of the words of the constraint itself ("our current supply", "supply allocation", "customer demand", "lead times") also names no product; otherwise the model's most likely objects for the allocation sentence would become product nodes. That word list serves this rule only (`names_object` and `generic_object` for the other predicates are unchanged).
  - The ticket's "we could experience supply constraints" is tested with the recorded 10-K's own sentence, which says "are experiencing and could, in the future, experience supply constraints"; the short phrase itself is in the unit test of the hedge check.
  - A company-level Claim's proposed layer is dropped with no `layer_reason` (the column's one value, `layer_unsupported`, would be wrong for it); `company_level` says why. A check constraint keeps a company-level edge from ever taking a layer.
  - The rule is applied again at review, to any Assertion: a researcher's `capacity_constrained` Assertion with no object, which was `not_eligible` `missing_object`, now forms the company-level edge when its subject is the filer. The other-company check is not repeated there.
  - `backend/atlas/investigations/` is untouched. The Editor, the Skeptic and the Financial Analyst are sent an empty object for a company-level Claim (their request models already allow it); their prompts are unchanged, so how the Editor words such a finding is unmeasured.
  - `backend/atlas/proposed_updates/detection.py` was touched (one line): a rejected company-level edge would otherwise be described as "... capacity_constrained None".
  - The limits recorded in the decision entry: the wide cue list with no object to anchor it leaves sentences such as "our backlog ... is not necessarily indicative" to the Reviewer; a gold case can't yet require a company-level edge specifically.
- **Next:** the lead re-chains `0057` (another ticket may hold `0056`); ticket 05's passage selection decides whether the allocation window is read at all; the reviewed pilot runs measure v8 and v5 and how many company-level Claims the wide cue list lets through.

## 2026-10-01: memory-directed reading ticket 11, Atlas marks its retains for the MiniMax extractor

- **Built** (`docs/decisions.md`, "Atlas's retains on MiniMax by metadata routing"; `docs/runbooks.md`, "Retains on MiniMax"):
  - **The setting.** `ATLAS_RETAIN_EXTRACTOR` (`retain_extractor`, `Literal["minimax"] | None`, default unset; an empty value is unset). When set, `atlas.retention.service.retain_item` adds `"extractor": "minimax"` to the item's metadata after the five provenance keys; unset, it builds the same item as before. Retention passes the setting through `Retention(extractor=)`, replays through `Replays(extractor=)`; evaluation runs retain through the same `retain` jobs with the case's settings (a copy of the base settings), so they follow it without a change.
  - **Recorded** (migration `0060`, `down_revision` `0055`): `memory_document.extractor` and `hindsight_operation.extractor` (text, null: none asked for, the primary), written at every submission (`Retention._submit`: the first retain, a resubmission after a quota or outage failure, a reprocess), so a section shows the extractor of its latest attempt. `GET /api/v1/source-versions/{id}/memory` returns `extractor` on each document and each operation. `provider_usage`'s provider check gains `hindsight_minimax`. The downgrade counts the routed operations against `codex` at their submission time, then drops the columns.
  - **Budgets** (`atlas.jobs.budget`). New provider `hindsight_minimax`, unit `operations`, budget `ATLAS_RETAIN_BUDGET_OPERATIONS` (`retain_budget_operations`, default 200). The kinds-by-provider mapping now depends on the setting: `provider_kinds(retain_extractor)`, and `Budgets` carries `retain_extractor` and `retain_operations` (`Budgets.providers()`, `.provider_of(kind)`, `.kinds(provider)`). Set: `retain`, `poll_operation` (still exempt) and `reprocess` are `hindsight_minimax` kinds and `codex` keeps `reflect`, `refresh_mental_model` and `replay`. Unset: `PROVIDER_KINDS` as before, and `hindsight_minimax` is not listed in `GET /api/v1/queue` or the budget metrics. An operation counts against the budget of the extractor it recorded (the `codex` sweep takes `extractor IS NULL` and the replay operations, the `hindsight_minimax` sweep `extractor = 'minimax'`), whatever the setting is when the queue counts it. The interactive reserve, the backfill rule and the window are the shared ones.
  - **Failure classification** (`atlas.jobs.pacing`): "no healthy deployments" and "no deployments available" in an operation's error are now `unavailable` (the prototype's failure text was `permanent` before). A relayed `RateLimitError … 429` was already `quota` and a relayed 503 already `unavailable`; a relayed 500 stays `permanent`.
  - **Docs:** the decision entry, the runbook section (the budget, switching on and back, what to check), `.env.example`, `AGENTS.md`, `docs/data-model.md` (§3.2, §3.3, §3.5a), the metric help text of `atlas_budget_used`.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/{settings.py,metrics.py}`, `backend/atlas/jobs/{budget.py,pacing.py,queue.py}`, `backend/atlas/retention/{service.py,handlers.py,reads.py}`, `backend/atlas/replay/{service.py,handlers.py}`, `backend/atlas/db/migrations/versions/0060_retain_extractor.py` (new), `frontend/lib/api/{openapi.json,schema.ts}` (regenerated), `tests/unit/test_settings.py`, `tests/integration/{test_retention,test_quota_pacing,test_queue_pause,test_replay,test_migrations}.py`, `.env.example`, `AGENTS.md`, `docs/{decisions,runbooks,data-model,implementation-log}.md`, the ticket.
- **Tests run (local, under WSL, one module at a time; actual results):** the first red run was `tests/unit/test_settings.py`, 3 failed (the default, the accepted values, the budget's lower bound) before the settings existed; the integration tests were written before the code but first run after it, so no red run of them was seen.
  - `tests/integration/test_retention.py` 16 passed (one new: with the setting, all five batches' items, the reprocess batch included, carry `extractor: minimax` after the five provenance keys, one item checked value by value, and every section and both operations of the 10-K record `minimax`; the existing batch test now also checks each item's fields and metadata keys in order, that no raw retain body contains `"extractor"`, and that sections and operations record null; a linked section records null).
  - `tests/integration/test_quota_pacing.py` 9 passed (three new: a retain run before the switch spends the one Codex operation, then with the setting on two of four retains are submitted and two held by `hindsight_minimax` while a queued `reflect` is held by `codex`, `GET /api/v1/queue` listing `codex`, `hindsight_minimax`, `minimax`, `tradingview` with the kinds of each, and the budget metrics under the provider name; a routed backfill stops at the backfill limit, an interactive retain runs in the reserve, the window rolls, and `codex` stays at 0; switched back, a held reprocess runs as a `codex` kind without the key, its section records null while the others keep `minimax`, and `codex` counts one operation, not two).
  - `tests/integration/test_queue_pause.py` 14 passed (three new cases of one test: a routed retain whose operation fails with a relayed rate limit pauses with `quota`, with "no healthy deployments" and with a relayed 503 `unavailable`; the sections stay pending, the operation records its class and `minimax`, and after the backoff the same items are resubmitted with the key and complete).
  - `tests/integration/test_replay.py` 16 passed (one new: with the setting, the replay bank's two batches carry the key and the replay's three operations count against `codex`, the research bank's routed retains not).
  - `tests/integration/test_migrations.py` 7 passed (head `0060`); `tests/integration/test_retention_triage.py` 14 passed; `tests/integration/test_tradingview.py` 6 passed; `tests/unit` 794 passed (two new settings tests and one new case).
  - `ruff format --check`, `ruff check` (backend, tests, scripts) and strict `pyright` (whole project): clean. Frontend (only the regenerated client changed): `typecheck` and `lint` clean, `test` 37 passed.
  - **Not run:** the other integration modules, the migration's downgrade (no test runs any downgrade), `npm run build`, the e2e, `scripts/ci.sh` and `scripts/gen_api_client.sh --check` (the client was regenerated with the script's two commands by hand): left to the runners.
- **Fixture vs live:** fixtures only. Hindsight is the recorded fake, which accepts any item metadata and routes nothing, so the tests show what Atlas sends, records and budgets, not that a Hindsight extracts on MiniMax. The three relayed error texts are hand-written: "no healthy deployments" after the text the lead's prototype saw on Hindsight 0.10.2 (its model name elided there), the rate limit and the 503 in the shape LiteLLM relays them; none was captured from a failing routed retain on the cluster. No call was made to Hindsight, LiteLLM or MiniMax for this ticket.
- **Deviations:**
  - `hindsight_operation` got an `extractor` column too (the ticket names the memory record only). The budget needs it: counting operations by the setting in force would re-count every recorded operation under the other provider, as spent now, when the setting changes.
  - Unset, `hindsight_minimax` is absent from the queue view and the metrics (the ticket says "shown like the others" for the set case and "unset, nothing changes"); operations routed before a switch back stay counted in `provider_usage` but aren't shown.
  - "Byte for byte" is tested as: every item's fields and metadata keys, in order, with their values, and no `"extractor"` in any raw retain body; not against a stored copy of the bytes (the document IDs are random per run).
  - A relayed 500 is not made an outage (the lead's note says "5xx"): 502/503/504 were and are; 500 stays `permanent` as before this ticket, since it can be the document's fault and a pause would then repeat for an hour at a time. Say so if it should pause.
  - An empty `ATLAS_RETAIN_EXTRACTOR` is accepted as unset (not asked for; it makes switching back possible by emptying the variable).
  - Replay documents (`replay_document`) don't record the extractor: the replay bank is deleted, and its operations stay on `codex` as the ticket says.
- **Next:** the lead re-chains `0060`. Before the setting is switched on in production: home-ops PR #7180 merged, then one retained section checked in Hindsight's LLM request log (the runbook's step 2). `ATLAS_RETAIN_BUDGET_OPERATIONS` 200 is unmeasured.
## 2026-10-01: memory-directed reading ticket 06, multi-hop Investigators

- **Built** (`docs/decisions.md`, "Multi-hop Investigators: the plan grows after the Scout"), on `integrate/mdr` (tickets 01–05 and 08):
  - **The ranking** (`atlas.investigations.companies`, new; the pure parts are `rank_companies` and `allot`). A company's weight is the sum of 1 / rank over the round's Scout pointers that name it (rank: the memory's place in its recall's results); ties by the better best rank, then by slug. Only companies with role `researched` are ranked (a counterparty never; a pointer with no company names nobody).
  - **The company budget.** Setting `ATLAS_INVESTIGATION_MAX_COMPANIES` (`investigation_max_companies`, default 6, 1–25): Investigators a round, the seeds counted. The request may set `budgets.max_companies` (1–25). Stored as `investigation.max_companies` (migration `0058`, `down_revision` `0055`; earlier investigations take 6; the column has no default afterwards). `Budgets` gained `max_companies` and `Usage` `companies` (the current round's Investigators); the evaluation runner and the Hypothesis dossier export pass the budget through.
  - **The plan grows** in `Investigations.advance` (`_grow`, `_add_investigators` in `atlas.investigations.service`), under the investigation's lock: on the first advance after the round's Scout has succeeded, which is in the transaction that records the Scout's outcome and before any Investigator is enqueued. Seeds keep their tasks. Each other ranked company gets an `investigator:<slug>` task in rank order while the budget has room: after the round's other Investigators (the later tasks' positions move down), depending on the Scout, with its own `company:<slug>` premise (inserted unless an earlier round left one) and artifacts `added_for_pointers`. The Skeptic's, the Analyst's and the Editor's `depends_on` gain the new keys, so they wait for every Investigator. The ranking is recorded once per round in the Scout task's artifacts (`pointed_companies` with each company's `outcome` `seed` / `added` / `no_room` / `premise_disproven`, `company_budget`, `investigators_added`, `companies_not_read`), which is also the marker that the round has grown (so resume and later advances do nothing more), and as one `companies_ranked` event. A follow-up round grows the same way from its own Scout's pointers. A round whose later tasks had already started (an investigation running across the upgrade) is left as it is.
  - **Document choice** (`TaskRunner._documents`): first the Source Versions the round's Scout pointers into the company name (one per Source Document, the latest a pointer names, by its best pointer), then the latest version of each of its other documents, newest first, within the task's share of the document budget; artifacts gain `documents_pointed`.
  - **Read side:** `GET /api/v1/investigations/{id}` gives `pointed_companies` (every round), `budgets.max_companies`, `usage.companies`; premises are ordered question, seeds, added companies. The research card gains `not_read` (`CardCompanyNotRead`: the pointed companies with no Investigator, with pointers, score, best rank and the reason), written by code in `atlas.investigations.coverage.not_read`; its `read` already had a row per Investigator task, so the added ones appear there. The Editor's request is unchanged in shape. The Financial Analyst is unchanged (seed companies only).
  - **Frontend:** the investigation page shows "Investigators N of M" in the budget table, a note on each added Investigator in the plan ("Added after the Scout: Memory points to AXT (4 pointers, best rank 1, weight 4)."), a table "Companies the pointers name" under Reading pointers (weight and what became of each) and "Companies not read" on the card; the workbench's start hint names the company budget. `frontend/lib/workbench.ts`: `pointerWeight`, `pointedOutcome`, `addedInvestigator`.
  - No prompt changed. `atlas.investigations.skeptic`, `atlas.investigations.pointers`, the claim checks and `atlas.claims.selection` are untouched.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/investigations/{companies.py (new),service.py,tasks.py,model.py,coverage.py,__init__.py}`, `backend/atlas/db/migrations/versions/0058_multi_hop_investigators.py` (new), `backend/atlas/api/investigations.py`, `backend/atlas/settings.py`, `backend/atlas/evaluation/runner.py`, `backend/atlas/hypotheses/export.py`, `.env.example`, `frontend/app/{investigation,workbench}/page.tsx`, `frontend/lib/{workbench.ts,api/client.ts,api/openapi.json,api/schema.ts}` (the last two regenerated), `frontend/unit/workbench.test.ts`, `tests/unit/test_pointed_companies.py` (new), `tests/fixtures/investigations/axt-supply-agreement.txt` (new, hand-shaped), `tests/integration/{test_investigations,test_investigation_follow_up,test_counterparties,test_migrations}.py`, `tests/live/test_live_verify.py`, `AGENTS.md`, `docs/{decisions,data-model,implementation-log}.md`, the ticket.

Four pointers at rank 1 into the note; the plan gains `investigator:axt` at position 4 with premise `company:axt` and `added_for_pointers` `{pointers: 4, score: 4.0, best_rank: 1}`; the Skeptic, the Analyst and the Editor depend on the three Investigators and are queued only after all three succeeded; the `companies_ranked` event and `pointed_companies` say why; AXT's Investigator reads the note and first the window holding the agreement (beyond the note's first 3,000 characters, selected by `pointer:0`–`pointer:3`); the Memory text reaches no role; the accepted Claim "AXT supplies Coherent" is in the Evidence tray and the card's finding; the card's `read` has rows for Coherent, Lumentum, AXT and the Skeptic and `not_read` is empty; the Financial Analyst's request names Coherent only; the chained review forms the edge AXT `supplies` Coherent.

    - with one seed and room for one more, the better-ranked of two pointed companies (Lumentum, retained first) is added and AXT is not read.

Along the way the no-room test first failed for a reason of the test's own: `import_notes`' two-line notes are not retained (their language is undetermined), so they have no fact and no pointer; the new tests use a memo in fuller English (`import_memo`, which asserts it was retained).

The API client was regenerated with the script's two commands (the CRLF checkout stops WSL running `scripts/gen_api_client.sh`).

- **Fixture-only vs live:** nothing live; no LLM, Hindsight, SEC or SearXNG call. Hindsight is the recorded fake, which recalls every in-scope fact for every query in retain order: the tests show the weight's arithmetic, the budget, the plan's growth, the document choice and the reading, not relevance. The AXT document and the memos are hand-written and labelled so; the Memory text of the acceptance test is the test's. Whether real recalls rank AXT first for the pilot's question, and what the added Investigators cost and yield, is for the pilot's re-run.
- **Deviations and readings of the ticket:**
  - **The acceptance test's third company has a hand-shaped document, not a recorded filing.** The recorded EDGAR fixtures hold filings of Coherent and Lumentum only (Nokia's fixture is XBRL facts), neither names the other in a relation, and recording an AXT filing needs a live SEC request. The ticket-01 test covers a non-seed company's recorded filings (Lumentum's, with Coherent the seed).
  - **The counterparty test can't make the role filter fail.** A counterparty can't be ingested or imported, so no pointer carries its company; the test shows that Memory naming it gives no Investigator. The SQL filter on `role = 'researched'` is a guard besides.
  - **A migration was needed** though the task table needs none: the request's company budget has to be stored with the investigation for the worker that later runs the Scout (`investigation.max_companies`).
  - **Not in the ticket:** `usage.companies`; the `premise_disproven` outcome (a non-seed whose premise is already disproven gets no Investigator and takes no room); the in-flight guard; premises ordered question, seeds, added; `documents_pointed`; one version per Source Document among the pointed ones (the latest a pointer names).

  - `not_read` is not sent to the Editor (no prompt or request change); it is on the card only.
  - `atlas.investigations.pointers` was not edited (ticket 07 edits it in parallel), so its module docstring still says "Companies and documents are not chosen by them yet (ticket 06)": one sentence to correct at integration.
- **Known limits** (decision entry): no rank cut-off and no normalisation in the weight; the document budget is not raised with the companies (six Investigators share 25 documents); each added Investigator has its own passage budget, so a six-Investigator round can spend three times the extraction tokens of a two-seed round, within the run's token budget; a pointed version that is no longer the latest of its document is read instead of the newer one.
- **Next:** ticket 07 (the Skeptic reads by pointers; its `reading_pointer` rows are kept out of the ranking and the document choice here by the `role = 'scout'` join). For production: `ATLAS_INVESTIGATION_MAX_COMPANIES` takes its default (6) unless home-ops sets it; consider the run token budget against six Investigators at 72 passages. The pilot re-run: read `pointed_companies` and each added task's `accepted`.
## 2026-10-01: memory-directed reading ticket 07, the Skeptic reads by pointers

- **Built** (`docs/decisions.md`, "The Skeptic reads where Memory points"; it amends "The Skeptic reads the archive: a deterministic fallback (pilot fix 06)"):
  - **Recalls.** Before its plan call the Skeptic's task asks Memory one query per bear-checklist item for each company the supporting Claims name (subjects, and object companies with role `researched`; never a counterparty), across the investigation's theme. The query is deterministic text (`atlas.roles.skeptic.bear_query`: `<company>: <the item's words>; <the Claims' objects, at most 4>`; each checklist item gained its `recall` words). At most `MAX_RECALL_COMPANIES` (6) companies, seeds first, so at most 36 recalls a task; no LLM call. The results are stored through `record_pointers` (now with an `actor`, here `atlas-skeptic`) as the task's reading pointers.
  - **Migration `0059_skeptic_pointers`** (`down_revision` `0055`, to be re-chained): `reading_pointer.query_kind` (`scout`, the default for rows recorded before, or `bear_checklist`), `checklist_item`, `query_company_id` (the company asked about), tied by the check `reading_pointer_bear_checklist_query`; a Skeptic's `query_index` is its query's position among its task's, from 1. The downgrade refuses while a bear-checklist pointer exists.
  - **`round_reading`** (same signature) returns only `query_kind = 'scout'` pointers, so an Investigator is never directed by a Skeptic's; `skeptic_pointers(connection, task_id)` reads a Skeptic task's.
  - **Documents:** the Tier A Source Versions the pointers lead to, best pointer first (`selected_by` `pointer`); then catalog matches of its search results (`search`, as before); then, for each named company no pointer document was chosen of, its latest 10-K and 10-Q as fix 06 chose them (`fallback`; the `skeptic_documents_fallback` event now names `company_ids`). Fix 06's "other theme companies' 10-Ks" step is gone. A follow-up round leaves out the documents an earlier round's Skeptic read.
  - **Passages:** `atlas.claims.selection.select` (pointer and search windows alternately, the floor and the ceiling), the search's terms the bear-checklist queries'; the Skeptic's `_windows` copy and its cue round-robin are removed. `Pointer` gained an optional `label` (additive), so a Skeptic passage's tag is `pointer:<checklist item>`. The checklist cues stay as the reading request's `checklist_items` hint (possibly empty); `skeptic.v3` and the contradiction/bear-context kinds are untouched.
  - **Plan:** `skeptic-plan.v5` (new file; v4 kept): queries only. `SkepticPlanRequest` lost `catalog` and `max_documents`, `SkepticPlan` lost `documents`. An empty plan searches nothing; a plan quarantined after its repair is recorded (`skeptic_plan_quarantined`) and the Skeptic reads on.
  - **Read side:** `pointers` on the investigation read carry `query_kind`, `checklist_item`, `query_company_id`, `query_company_name` (the Skeptic's after the Scout's of the round); the card's Skeptic `read` row gives each document's `selected_by` (`pointer` | `search` | `fallback`, `plan` on older cards) and `selections`; the Skeptic task's artifacts gain `documents_from_pointers`, `passages_by_selection`, `recall_companies`, `recall_companies_dropped` and the pointer counts; the Research Snapshot's `reading_pointers` carry the new columns and `memory.skeptic_pointer_selections` lists the Skeptic's pointer-chosen passages. The investigation page lists the Skeptic's pointers under "The Skeptic's", by checklist item and company.
  - **Docstrings and rule:** "No Memory is sent or read" is now "No Memory is sent to the Skeptic; Memory chooses what it reads" (`atlas.investigations.skeptic`, `tasks.py`, `pointers.py`, `roles/skeptic.py`, the page).
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/investigations/{skeptic,pointers,tasks,model,coverage,__init__}.py`, `backend/atlas/roles/skeptic.py`, `backend/atlas/roles/prompts/skeptic-plan.v5.md` (new), `backend/atlas/claims/selection.py` (the `label`), `backend/atlas/db/migrations/versions/0059_skeptic_pointers.py` (new), `backend/atlas/snapshots.py`, `backend/atlas/evaluation/stubs.py`, `backend/atlas/api/investigations.py` (docstring), `frontend/app/investigation/page.tsx`, `frontend/lib/{workbench.ts,api/openapi.json,api/schema.ts}` (the last two regenerated), `frontend/unit/workbench.test.ts`, `frontend/e2e/workbench.spec.ts`, `scripts/{e2e,e2e_hypothesis}.py`, `tests/live/verify.py`, `tests/unit/{test_bottleneck_prompts,test_passage_selection,test_skeptic_checks}.py`, `tests/integration/{test_investigations,test_investigation_follow_up,test_hypotheses,test_proposed_updates,test_scenarios,test_edgar_discovery,test_migrations}.py`, `AGENTS.md`, `CONTEXT.md`, `docs/{decisions,evaluation-methodology,implementation-log}.md`, the ticket.
- **Tests** (WSL, the Compose Postgres and Silo, one module at a time; actual results):

The old fallback test (`…plan_that_chooses_no_document_falls_back…`) is replaced by the fallback test above. `finding_nothing` and `skeptic_plan` answer the v5 shape.
  - **Other modules' helpers** answer the v5 plan and tell the plan call from the reading call by `"passages" in request` (the `catalog` field is gone): `test_hypotheses.py`, `test_proposed_updates.py`, `test_scenarios.py`, `test_investigation_follow_up.py`, `test_edgar_discovery.py` (its Skeptic test gives the 10-K no fact so the filing search adds it). `test_investigation_follow_up.py`: round 2's Skeptic now reads Lumentum's note through the fallback (one passage, one more Skeptic call, 500 more tokens in the total), round 1's reads by pointer. `test_hypotheses.py`: the snapshot test also checks the pointers' new columns and `skeptic_pointer_selections`.
  - **Unit:** `test_skeptic_checks.py` (`bear_query`: the phrasing, each object once, at most four; every item has distinct recall words), `test_passage_selection.py` (a labelled pointer's tag), `test_bottleneck_prompts.py` (`skeptic-plan` v5's text; the plan's request and response fields). **Migration:** `test_reading_pointers_recorded_before_0059_are_the_scout_s` (a row inserted at `0055` is `scout`; a bear-checklist row needs its item and company and a Scout's may have neither; the table still refuses UPDATE); the head is `0059`.
  - **Red runs.** The three new seam tests were run against the old code first (with only the test helpers changed): all failed (the old plan schema rejects `{"queries": []}`, and nothing recalled for the Skeptic); the run's `-k` pattern also caught the Investigator's pointer test, which failed for the helper change: 4 failed. The unit, migration and frontend tests were written with their code; no red run of them was seen.

Frontend: `lint` and `typecheck` clean, `test` 38 passed. `ruff format --check`, `ruff check` (backend, tests, scripts) and strict `pyright` (whole project, 0 errors): clean. The API client was regenerated with the script's two commands. After those runs one typing tidy was made in the Skeptic's artifacts (no behaviour change); ruff and pyright were run again, and the two acceptance tests (2 passed), not the whole modules.

- **Deviations and readings of the ticket:**
  - **The acceptance memory is derived from the recorded 10-K, not a 10-Q:** the recorded Coherent and Lumentum 10-Qs hold a cover and financial statements only and state no customer concentration.
  - **"The Claims of the round"** is read as the supporting Claims the Skeptic is sent (the run's accepted Claims, every round's): a follow-up round's Skeptic asks about earlier rounds' companies too.

  - **The plan request lost `catalog` and `max_documents`** as well as the response's `documents` ("change the schema only as needed": the prompt no longer describes them, and the catalog was up to 60 documents of tokens). Every helper that told the plan call by `"catalog" in request` was changed; a test written in parallel that still does will see its plan answer quarantined (and the Skeptic read on).
  - **`selected_by` of a Skeptic passage is `pointer:<checklist item>`**, through an additive `label` on `selection.Pointer`; the company asked about is on the pointer row, not in the tag. The passages are in `skeptic_search.passages` (and the snapshot); the API shows them as counts (`selections`, `passages_by_selection`), as before.
  - **The evaluation gold cases are untouched** (they are pinned by hash): a case's `skeptic_plan.documents` is now ignored by the stub, and `docs/evaluation-methodology.md` says so. EV-CON-001 still passes (`test_evaluations.py`): under the stub's empty recall the Skeptic reads the fallback's documents.
  - **The Research Snapshot** gained `skeptic_pointer_selections` (not in the ticket; the snapshot's docstring promises the pointer choices are listed).
  - `.claude/skills/pilot-review/SKILL.md` still says "a Skeptic that only searched read nothing"; it was left as it is (as ticket 03 left it).
- **Next:** the lead re-chains `0059` and runs CI with the e2e. With ticket 06, `round_reading` keeps its signature and now returns the Scout's pointers only; a new Investigator test that scripts the Skeptic should use the v5 plan shape. The pilot re-run: the Skeptic's `pointers` by checklist item and rank, `passages_by_selection`, `documents_fallback`, and whether the bear context found is on the checklist; then decide the recall cap, a rank cut-off, and whether the Skeptic should read transcripts.

## 2026-10-02: memory-directed reading integrated; release 0.3.0

## 2026-10-02: memory-quality ticket 16, the Editor's card fits a multi-hop investigation

- **Built** (`docs/decisions.md`, "A cut-off answer is not a schema failure; the card never goes missing"):
  - **Truncated outcome (every role).** `RoleCaller.call_recorded` reads the completion's `finish_reason`; an answer that fails validation with `finish_reason == "length"` or `completion_tokens >= max_tokens` closes the role call `truncated` and raises `RoleOutputTruncated` (a subclass of `RoleOutputQuarantined`, so every existing `except RoleOutputQuarantined` path is unchanged) without sending a repair. A valid answer is used whatever its tokens. `call_recorded` takes an optional `max_output_tokens` for one call. `RoleCallStatus` gains `truncated`; **migration `0061_role_call_truncated`** (`down_revision` `0060`) widens `role_call_status_check`; the downgrade records truncated calls as `quarantined`.
  - **The Editor's cap.** `EDITOR.max_output_tokens` 4,096 → 8,192; setting `ATLAS_EDITOR_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` (`editor_max_output_tokens`, default 16,384) is the bound; a truncated card answer is asked once more with `min(2 × cap, bound)`; the run's token budget still caps each request.
  - **Short references (`editor.v6`, new file, LF; v5 kept).** The card request's Claims are `EditorCardClaim` with `ref` `c1`…`cN` (the quotes' `retrieved_data` ids are the refs too); contradictions carry `contradicts_refs`; findings answer `claim_refs` (`CardFindingDraft`). Code maps refs back to Claim IDs; an unknown ref makes the finding unsupported like an unknown ID ("cites what isn't an accepted Claim of this investigation: c2"; its `claim_ids` record the known refs as Claim IDs and the rest as written). The Hypothesis Editor (`EditorClaim`, `EditorFinding`, `editor-hypothesis.v3`) is unchanged. **Chosen: refs only, not both forms**: the scripted answers were few and mechanical to move (below).
  - **Fallback card.** If the Editor is truncated at the bound or quarantined, the Editor task succeeds with a code-written card: no finding, `editor_verdict` `needs_review`, new fields `editor_failure` and `claims_by_company` (`CardCompanyClaims`/`CardClaimSummary`), `editor_role_call_id` the failed call, plus the usual contradictions, bear context, searched, read, not_read. Stop `needs_review` ("the Editor failed, so the card has no finding: …"), or `no_new_independent_evidence` with no accepted Claim. The investigation page shows the reason and the Claims by company (`EditorFailed` in `frontend/app/investigation/page.tsx`).
  - **Fake:** `ChatReply` has a `finish_reason` (default `stop`; `ChatReply.text(..., finish_reason="length")`).
- **Scripted Editor answers moved to short references: 10 responders in 10 files**: `tests/integration/{test_investigations (editing() and the lead-citing test's findings), test_investigation_follow_up, test_edgar_discovery, test_scenarios, test_hypotheses (card_editor; the shared `claim_ids_by_quote` helper takes `claim_id` or `ref`), test_proposed_updates (card_editor; shared `claim_ids` helper likewise)}.py`, `backend/atlas/evaluation/stubs.py` (`_editor`), `tests/live/verify.py` (rehearsal), `scripts/e2e.py`, `scripts/e2e_hypothesis.py` (card_editor and its helper). The gold cases needed no change (their editor scripts hold no IDs; the stub computes citations), so their pinned hashes are untouched. Assertions changed too: the Editor request's `ref`/`contradicts_refs`/retrieved ids in `test_investigations.py`, prompt version 5 → 6 there, in `test_hypotheses.py` and `test_bottleneck_prompts.py`, the migration head `0061`.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/roles/{caller,records,editor,__init__}.py`, `backend/atlas/roles/prompts/editor.v6.md` (new), `backend/atlas/investigations/{tasks,model}.py`, `backend/atlas/db/migrations/versions/0061_role_call_truncated.py` (new), `backend/atlas/settings.py`, `.env.example`, `backend/atlas/evaluation/stubs.py`, `frontend/app/investigation/page.tsx`, `frontend/lib/api/{openapi.json,schema.ts}` (regenerated), `tests/fakes/litellm.py`, the test and script files above, `tests/integration/{test_role_calls,test_migrations}.py`, `AGENTS.md`, `docs/{decisions,implementation-log}.md`, the ticket.
- **Tests** (WSL, the shared Postgres and Silo; actual results):
  - **New:** `test_role_calls.py`: `test_an_answer_cut_off_at_the_output_cap_is_truncated_without_a_repair` (two cases: `finish_reason` `length`; tokens equal to the cap), `test_a_complete_answer_at_the_cap_is_used_and_one_call_may_have_a_larger_cap`. `test_investigations.py`: `test_an_editor_answer_cut_off_at_its_cap_is_asked_again_with_a_larger_cap` (90 accepted Claims; the Editor's first answer cut at 8,192 → `truncated`, no repair, retried at 16,384 → accepted; refs `c1`…`c90` sent, no Claim ID in the request; the finding cites all 90 Claim IDs); `test_an_editor_that_still_fails_leaves_a_card_without_findings_and_needs_review` (two cases: cut off at the bound, quarantined; stop `needs_review`, the fallback card read back with `editor_failure`, `claims_by_company`, `searched`, `read`, the Evidence tray unchanged); `test_a_role_s_answer_cut_off_at_its_cap_is_truncated_and_fails_as_a_quarantine_did` (the Scout cut off three times: three `truncated` calls of one attempt each, the task fails after 3 attempts, stop `needs_review`, as a quarantine did). The unknown-reference case extends `test_an_editor_finding_that_cites_no_accepted_claim_is_dropped_and_needs_review` (`c2` when only `c1` was sent).
  - **No red run was made**: the new tests were written with the code and passed on their first run (5 passed in the first `-k` run; `test_role_calls.py` 22 passed).
